# Architecture Optimization Execution Record

## R4: local evidence and remaining acceptance (2026-10-10)

P8.3 coverage-trend and gate review is complete: **37 I / 14 P / 1 U** across the original 52 items, or **71.2% fully completed items**. This is not a workload estimate or whole-plan completion. R1–R3 technical cleanup is complete; current R4 evidence, removed/retained entries and all 15 remaining items are centralized in the [final candidate report](final_acceptance_2026_10_09.md).

The single full-suite/coverage attempt has **4,951 passes, 2 existing skips and 1 source-initialization five-second timeout**. The unchanged failing test passed alone with the same assertions/deadline; the failed full-run record remains and no additional full suite was run. LCOV is **40,198/50,542 (79.53%)**. Strict analysis, formatting of 1,031 Dart files, 116 Python checks (3 existing platform skips) and the remaining gates pass. Windows/Android builds, complete artifact hashes and synthetic-signing limits are in the same report.

Inventory: **524 libraries / 330 business / 194 UI / 0 pending / 245 entries**, unchanged 57 feature edges and **42 UI/navigation cycle members**. Business code is acyclic and cannot reach UI. Declared SDK, complete CLI, five-platform/performance and historical-recovery acceptance remains in the original items; P8.5 stays P as required by the plan.

## R3 second group: default stores and view dependencies (2026-10-09)

History and favorite defaults are read-only. The 200 default-registry assignments in 45 legacy fixtures have been removed. Favorite views use an explicit store and captured binding; readers retain history/image-favorite owners, while import facades and follow-update jobs receive their actual services/managers. Generation, path, original-view and queued-admission checks remain. Whole-application import fixtures still exercise real default close/reopen behavior without resetting the registry.

P8.1 closes: **36 I / 15 P / 1 U** across the original 52 items. Inventory: **524 libraries / 330 business / 194 UI / 0 pending / 245 business entries**, with unchanged allowed edges. R3 technical cleanup is complete; P8.3 full coverage trends belong to R4. This group used related tests and affected deltas, without a full suite, coverage or release build. Overlapping run counts are not additive. Commands, original failures and input binding are in the [batch report](completion_batches_2026_10_09.md) and [single R3 audit](architecture_compatibility_completion_2026_10_09.md).

The final UI/navigation file cycle has **42 members** after the favorite-settings view leaves the previous 43-member cycle.

## R3 first group: responsibilities and compatibility entries (2026-10-09)

All 28 pending libraries have a full responsibility review. Inventory is **522 files: 330 business / 192 UI / 0 pending, 245 business entries**. The original 46-file SCC becomes 43 individually explained UI/navigation files; business cycles are absent, with all 57 feature edges and previous business protections retained. See the [single R3 audit](architecture_compatibility_completion_2026_10_09.md).

## R1 first group: favorite entries and atomic bulk addition (2026-10-09)

Quick deletion/export, bulk addition, network import, remote deletion/folder creation and local folder opening now retain their original caller and target. ## P2/P4/P6/P7/P8: Original ownership of favorite transfers and metadata refresh (2026-10-09)

- Final delivery: Windows release built in 124.1s; packaged CHANGELOG/init.js/translation.json match source. app.so SHA-256 `B5815C35D9BBC87D10DB5D740FB9BBA9908C429236050F57717EEB0927C10375`. `favorite-workflow-ownership-artifact-hashes.json` binds source, validation, artifacts and commit.

- Baseline 0f52561c. After fixing the missing MenuButton fixture import, the original transfer implementation produced **3 passes / 27 failures**. After supplying the Fake source's enableTagsTranslate property, unchanged metadata production code produced **2 passes / 17 failures**; transfer edits were already present. Initial fixture/compiler failures and all snapshots remain separate from product defects.
- favorite_transfer_dialog.dart captures the original folder, detached comics and selection. Presentation and buttons check the original caller/route/host; actual AppDataOperations admission rechecks manager identity, connection generation, path and source/destination folders. Accepted valid writes remain with their original host. Publication waits for both presentation and the complete save callback. Successful transfers now pop the owning outer route, fixing the old inner-Navigator dismissal.
- Success and committed/unknown persistence outcomes are never replayed by the same confirmation; notCommitted and ordinary errors permit explicit retry. A real SQLite observer failure verifies that adding the same comic again and pressing OK cannot move the new record. Failed pop after success retries only dismissal. Unassigned added and unused selectedLocalFolders fields and their ineffective disabled branches are removed; New Folder reuses the existing entry.
- FavoriteMetadataUpdate injects loading, saving, admission and progress. It detaches inputs, processes batches of four, awaits every accepted read, attempts ordinary loads at most three times and saves each loaded item once. Cancelled/unsupported results do not retry as ordinary failures. Res.failure is unpacked inside RequestScope.runToCompletion so a genuine late error survives cancellation. Already-started saves finish with their known commit state; a failed progress callback cancels later batches while draining accepted reads.
- Metadata presentation retains the original manager, generation, path, folder and source instances/loaders. Retries never switch sources. LocalFavoritesPage owns cancellation on disposal/folder changes, while the original application/WindowFrame drains real source completion and presentation. The stale returned-list assignment is removed; original manager notifications refresh the page. Removal and late source failures remain together, explicit retry only removes the original route, and ValueNotifier disposal waits for both task and route. Empty progress is finite and cancellation is distinct; only two Chinese Cancelled translations are added.
- **79 new regressions**: 34 transfer, 25 metadata UI, 17 service and 3 native synthetic QuickJS. Final targeted run: **656 passes**. Real SQLite checks per-item failure without load/save replay, committed observer failure, (id,type)/rowid, original time/order/translated tags/tracking fields, other identities/folders, network links and reopening. Real parser integration checks native reference release, cancelled Promise draining and late rejection. Real WindowFrame tests cover accepted transfer/read completion and shutdown failure. No real application or personal data was opened.
- An implementation edit accidentally removed adjacent _checkExitSelectMode and was restored exactly. Supplemental fixture mistakes called nonexistent reorderFolders and looked up a disposed navigator; the first repair targeted the wrong occurrence. All logs remain. The 19 early analyzer diagnostics were fixed without new skips, relaxed timeouts or exclusions. Final formatting and full format check precede the freeze of **15 changed paths and all 1020 Dart files**. Full run: **4844 passes / 2 existing skips**, LCOV **39,747/50,169 (79.23%)**. Strict analysis is clean; Python 110 tests / 3 existing skips and structure/architecture/version/Git-dependency gates pass.
- Full review registers one business service/entry and two UI adapters: **520 files, 326 business, 166 UI, 28 pending, 241 entries**. 91 baseline blobs, 57 feature edges and the original 46-file SCC are unchanged; 12 reverse probes are rejected. Existing repository SQL/schema/transactions, parsers, request/window primitives, other favorite actions and public JS/CLI contracts remain intact. See [compatibility](favorite_workflow_ownership_compatibility.md).
- The 52-item status remains **24 I / 27 P / 1 U**; 46.2% measures implemented items. Quick favorite deletion/export/addFavorite/network import/external opening, pending responsibilities/SCC semantics, domain-wide interfaces/settings/startup/resources/storage/source/account contracts, compatibility retirement, complete CLI, declared SDK 3.41.4, all five platforms and six fixed-device performance scenarios remain open. The sole remaining failure after fixture correction was an actual 200px large-text narrow-window overflow; only this dialog's actions change to Flexible/Wrap. Busy accessible naming and ordinary ContentDialog layout remain. Early unused-import/brace diagnostics were fixed without extra skips or weaker timeouts.

- Final delivery: Windows release built in 96.2s; packaged CHANGELOG/init.js/translation.json match source. app.so SHA-256 `3D3032AA25FF5616DA9AE3107821CDB07950A4DABB3CD7ECA7C1F2309BCC0A52`. `favorite-comic-order-artifact-hashes.json` binds source, validation, artifacts and commit.

SettingsSaveState adds mayLeaveSettingsAfterSaveFailure, default false; only retired comic-reorder targets opt in. Dismissal does not acknowledge or clear failures: the original host retains the same error/stack and no write is replayed.

## P4/P5/P8: Reader sidebar cleanup and original hosts (2026-10-09)

- Final delivery: Windows release built in 97.3s; packaged CHANGELOG/init.js/translation.json match source. app.so SHA-256 `F2557A7BBBDD2B1FD792A86234A0293F30F85AACDB5A586AAC5C64BB4D7989C5`. `reader-sidebar-cleanup-artifact-hashes.json` binds source, validation, artifacts and commit.

- Baseline `c2cda65`: all 10 new reader-sidebar cases fail with unchanged production, covering lost cleanup retries, premature host exit, replaced hosts and reentrant pause acquisition. The first repair has 120 targeted passes/one failure. A separate case then reproduces duplicate cleanup during one window close against the unchanged shared WindowSelectionTask. Its first teardown did not drain; the log is retained and the fixture now uses the existing settling helper. The corrected baseline ends with zero passes/one genuine failure. No timeout or skip was relaxed.
- ReaderSidebarBinding reuses WindowSelectionTask, registering the original application/window before acquiring the pause and retaining the nearest Navigator/request. Failed removal ends presentation waiting but preserves the original error/stack and route. Handles, binding.close, repeated dispose and host shutdown can retry; completed routes and borrowed releases are not replayed. - Baseline `baad85f`. Two native regressions reproduced one retained nested-Promise reference until engine closure and completion being reported before descendants settled. Display and drain now complete independently: objects containing Promises still display immediately. The 30-second deadline only bounds top-level display waiting; it does not cancel the shared engine or arbitrary script work.
- drainJsResultDescendants observes Futures in returned/rejected graphs after synchronous release. Identity tracking spans map keys/values, cycles, repeated Futures and aliased references across multiple levels; siblings release independently as they settle. The full root is seeded before subscriptions so synchronous Futures cannot release root aliases twice. Failed native frees are not retried through aliases. Original errors/stacks, synchronous release errors and all later cleanup failures remain available. Nested rejection does not overwrite displayed output; completion remains observable and detached pages still log failures. Runtime closure cancels its pending descendants without affecting sibling engines.
- Eighteen new regressions: six result-graph, five pure execution, four native and three synchronous-injection/page-lifecycle tests; 84 extended tests pass. A separate native probe records zero references after completion, previously one, and zero after closure. A plain Dart process compares 12 display samples with original formatting and verifies independent drain waiting. Both pre-fix failures remain in logs. Timeouts, existing skips and dependency versions are unchanged. The JsEngine class and subsequent implementation are textually unchanged; synchronous release reuses shared traversal. Public JS APIs and source formats remain unchanged.
- Eight source/test paths and all 940 Dart paths are frozen. Full suite: **4031 passed, 2 existing skips** (concurrency 4), LCOV **35,803/48,089 (74.45%)**. Strict analysis has zero diagnostics; eight-file formatting, Python104/3 existing skips, structure/architecture, version and Git dependency checks pass. All dependency protections, 57 feature edges, 232 business entries, 500-file classification (317 business/124 UI/59 pending) and the 47-file SCC are unchanged.
- Original 52 remain **24 I / 27 P / 1 U**. This unit drains descendants in transferred result graphs. Unreturned timers/tasks and arbitrary later mutations to Dart containers are outside this contract. Remaining file classification, navigation-cycle semantics, domain interfaces/configuration/lifetimes/storage/JS/compatibility, full CLI, declared SDK3.41.4, five platforms and fixed-device performance still require acceptance. ## P2/P4/P7: JS compute ownership, shutdown draining and cycle removal (2026-10-07)

- Final delivery: Windows release built in 95.0s; packaged CHANGELOG/init.js/translation.json match source. app.so SHA-256 `C2B9E64002A3B21CA02918B36701372A290577A034931522C2C89E5108AA9693`. Seven frozen paths match the final full suite. Flutter 3.41.6 / Dart 3.11.4 does not replace declared-SDK or five-platform acceptance. js-compute-owner-artifact-hashes.json binds source, validation, artifacts and commit.

- Baseline `fba3bf8`. Two real Windows QuickJS probes failed before this change: independent-engine compute lost its custom initialization script, and closeAndWait returned before accepted compute finished. Each JsEngine now lazily owns its own JSPool and copies its actual initialization image. Workers and nested compute use that image; closing one engine does not close another engine's workers. Unused engines create no pool.
- The global JSPool and implicit default factory are removed. The pool schedules workers/ports; runtime bootstrap lives in js_engine.dart with explicit factory/entry injection. Workers await engine.closeAndWait before their stop acknowledgement, including nested pools and native/HTTP release. Core registers the original JS engine, which owns pool shutdown. The **js_engine.dart ↔ js_pool.dart cycle is removed**. Business roots grow **177→179**, with two new acyclic constraints. The roots reach 31/19 project files and no registered UI; no SCC is added/enlarged and allowed feature edges/UI registration are unchanged. App/AppData/Log is the only remaining cycle reachable from enforced business roots; this is not a whole-project acyclicity claim.
- Shutdown rejects JS delivery before waiting for original work, pool startup and worker cleanup. Partial startup can retry after cleanup; failed shutdown retains its pool, original errors and stable closing Future while awaiting siblings. A further failing regression reproduced initialization returning early when both initialization and synchronous HTTP close failed. Cleanup now waits for the compute pool and preserves the initialization cause plus both cleanup causes. The constructor documents that every worker evaluates the initialization image; unconditional top-level compute recursively initializes more workers. Production assets/init.js only defines the compute API. Test script-cache hooks remain; full error classification and reset lifecycle need further review.
- Added **11 regressions**. The earlier expanded suite passed 44; the strengthened ownership/lifecycle/core suite passed 25. Final full suite: **3914 passed, 2 existing skips**, LCOV **35,422/47,900 (73.95%)**. Strict analysis has zero diagnostics; six-file formatting, Python 83 tests with 3 existing skips, structure/architecture, release and Git dependency checks pass. Windows QuickJS covers isolated scripts, nested compute and real core shutdown; controlled worker delays/failures do not prove every platform's HTTP or process-exit behavior.
- One initial braces lint was fixed. The first full run was intentionally terminated after verified process identification when formatting reported a line-wrap difference; it is not a pass. Earlier freezes/logs remain. Final seven-path source/test/baseline evidence is js-compute-owner-frozen-sources-v3.json and full-v3. Original 52 items remain **24 I / 27 P / 1 U**; 46.2% is implementation-evidence share. The foundation cycle, P3 full configuration, P4 complete lifecycle, P5 platform behavior, P6 recovery/all writers, P7/P8, full CLI, declared SDK, five platforms and fixed-device performance remain open.

## P2/P4/P7: cache validation ownership and network dependency cycle removal (2026-10-07)

- Final delivery: Windows release built in 95.5s; packaged CHANGELOG/init.js/translation.json match source. app.so SHA-256 `97A5216EDD12071272871D33FC3A6BE5ADB7174F80EE8842B3AC24FC11FB1FC0`. Six frozen paths match the final full suite. Flutter 3.41.6 / Dart 3.11.4 does not replace declared-SDK or five-platform acceptance. cache-owner-artifact-hashes.json binds source, tests, dependency graphs, artifacts and commit.

- Baseline `d6a156e`. Two pre-fix regressions fail: cache HEAD bypasses the original adapter, and changed Authorization reuses another credential's cache. NetworkCacheManager now owns shared data only; each AppDio installs its own NetworkCacheInterceptor. HEAD uses the original client, complete interceptor chain, current adapter and cancellation token, without another AppDio. Headers are captured before cookie/authentication processing, so replay avoids duplicated cookies while preserving HEAD response cookie updates.
- JSON response validation moves to network/json_response.dart with direct imports and no app_dio re-export. The **app_dio.dart ↔ cache.dart file cycle is removed**. Three roots and three acyclic constraints increase enforced business entries **174→177**. AppDio/cache/JSON reach 28/2/1 project files without registered UI or added/enlarged SCC. Allowed feature edges and UI registration are unchanged. Foundation/JS cycles remain; this is not a claim that the whole project is free of business cycles.
- HEAD consumes its byte response without invoking GET progress callbacks. Failures preserve original causes/stacks and complete the original GET; 405/501 fall through to one GET. Cancellation cannot fall through or serve cached output. Client close and OwnedDioClient draining cover HEAD, late bodies and cleanup failures on the same adapter. Cleared/replaced cache entries cannot supply old validation output, and an old failure cannot delete a replacement. Header names compare case-insensitively; Authorization/token and response representation participate in cache matching.
- A separate final-review probe reproduced a missing GET-body cache identity on v1: it returned an unrelated cached result. Payload-bearing GETs, including single-subscription streams, now bypass both lookup and insertion; only the actual GET sends their payload, with no HEAD or pollution of the bodyless cache. The first full suite was intentionally terminated after launcher identity checks at +2294 and is not a pass. Its logs and v1 freeze/audit remain; final evidence uses cache-owner-frozen-sources-v2.json.
- Added **20 regressions**. Final full suite: **3903 passed, 2 existing skips**, LCOV **35,395/47,883 (73.92%)**. The earlier network/favorite suite passed 198 tests; final targeted tests plus the independent payload probe pass 21. Three real Windows RHttp/loopback HTTP cases cover matching validation, changed content followed by GET, and closing the original client during HEAD, checking adapter method sequences and waitForIdle. Late-body close/cancel failures remain with the owner. Test VMs do not replace full-app or five-platform validation. Strict analysis has zero diagnostics; formatting five Dart files, Python 83 tests with 3 existing skips, structure/architecture, release and Git dependency checks pass.
- Original 52 items remain **24 I / 27 P / 1 U**; 46.2% is implementation-evidence share. Shared-cache lifecycle, complete HTTP caching/invalidation behavior, in-flight GET repopulation after clear, foundation/JS cycles, P3 configuration, P4 full shutdown, P5 platform behavior, P6 recovery/all writers, P7/P8, full CLI, declared SDK, five platforms and fixed-device performance remain open. Persistent layouts and dependency versions are unchanged.

## P6/P7: instance isolation and recovery for uncertain relocation (2026-10-07)

- Final delivery: Windows release built in 94.9s; packaged CHANGELOG/init.js/translation.json match source. app.so SHA-256 `5A38AED3362A9AF16067FE4B17E0D6A9F8952C3376B8C7E21D7131850362D9B7`. Twelve frozen paths match the final full suite and 12 interruption checks. Flutter 3.41.6 / Dart 3.11.4 does not replace declared-SDK or five-platform acceptance. library-authority-artifact-hashes.json binds source, logs, screenshots, artifacts and commit.

- Baseline `c960282`. When COMMIT succeeds but both its acknowledgement and the following journal read fail, the old instance could still insert records, expose the old root, allocate downloads there and read cached covers. Four pre-fix regressions failed. A later regression separately reproduced directory creation overlapping a move. Database/path/model access and new import/download admission now check the owning instance; allocation retains a local-file reservation until its actual Future finishes, rejects concurrent relocation as busy, and rechecks the original instance after waiting.
- **No schema, existing key or persistent-layout change in this batch**; version-1 local_storage_relocation is reused. Each manager retains source/destination and whether commit has started, entering isolation before preparation. Only autocommit permits a durable journal read; prepared state selects the source and committed state selects the destination. Commit returns the confirmed complete state so publishing path/model mappings does not reread the journal. Explicit recovery retries ROLLBACK on the original unsettled connection; repeated failure preserves isolation and both original/secondary errors.
- A missing journal can restore the source only when this attempt has not started commit and the source still exists. After commit starts, missing or changed root evidence and missing authoritative directories keep access blocked. Prepared recovery publishes the confirmed source before forgetting the journal, avoiding proofless isolation after failed deletion acknowledgement. Once committed authority is known, mirror/control cleanup failures retain known access and retry evidence. Recovery alone neither moves again nor clears old payload. Isolation belongs to the original manager/connection; another manager or reopened connection cannot release its old models.
- App settings extracts LocalStorageSettings. Uncertain storage shows a persistent recovery status, disables copying and offers Recover Local Storage / Retry against the original connection without another directory picker. SettingsTaskPresenter owns completion, refreshes after success or failure, and avoids updating an unmounted page. Simplified/traditional Chinese and a live region are included. Six UI tests pass; three final Microsoft YaHei/Material Icons screenshots cover 375×667@1, 667×375@3.2 dark and 1024×768@2. Component screenshots do not prove full-app/platform behavior.
- Added **26 regressions** (20 instance/storage and 6 settings). Final full suite: **3883 passed, 2 existing skips**; LCOV **35,298/47,863 (73.75%)**. The final allocation checks passed 63 targeted tests; strict analysis has zero diagnostics, 10 Dart files format unchanged, Python 83 tests with 3 existing skips, release and Git dependency checks pass. Earlier fake fields/one-shot fault hooks, toast targeting, Semantics disposal and icon-font fixtures were corrected, retaining failed logs instead of treating intermediate results as final evidence.
- Final **12 independent test-VM interruption scenarios** add unknown commit, failed preparation/commit rollback and failed recovery read to the eight prepared, row UPDATE, COMMIT, mirror rename, completion/intent/receipt deletion and journal deletion checkpoints. Four new scenarios verify isolation and rejection of late add before exit(86). Independent restart checks full rows, pages, covers/getImages, cleared journals and retained old payload. Flutter parent exits 79. Final allocation changes use library-authority-frozen-sources-v2.json and library-authority-crash-v2-audit.json; v1 is retained as superseded evidence. These are Windows test VMs, not power-loss/full-app/SAF proof.
- Enforced business roots remain **174**. Manager/migration/journal/import service reach 140/96/94/144 project files with no registered UI and no added/enlarged file SCC. Only the new settings UI is registered; allowed edges, acyclic constraints and business roots are unchanged. Foundation/JS/network cycles remain. Twelve source/test/translation/baseline paths are frozen. Original 52 items stay **24 I / 27 P / 1 U**; 46.2% is implementation-evidence share, not P6/P4 or overall acceptance.
- Historical broken paths without journals, incomplete-copy continuation/explicit cleanup, legacy intents/damaged receipts/registration conflicts, multi-store unknown COMMIT/failed DETACH and old partial rows remain open. Already captured File objects and active reads are not revoked or relocated. All cross-process writers, external path races, real SAF, power-loss/fsync, remaining P2–P8, full CLI, declared SDK, five platforms and fixed-device performance still require work.

## P6: library directory commit and startup recovery (2026-10-07)

- Final delivery: Windows release built in 97.8s; packaged CHANGELOG/init.js/translation.json match source. app.so SHA-256 `842F80B6C720E3724FF8BB2627D93545F4DA334C78BACD5E4EEB341368318691`. Seven frozen paths match the full suite and eight interruption checks. Flutter 3.41.6 / Dart 3.11.4 does not replace declared-SDK or five-platform acceptance. library-relocation-artifact-hashes.json binds source, logs, artifacts and commit.

- Baseline `8d34839`. Real copy import followed by setNewPath moved files but left absolute directory rows pointing at the emptied library; the original external probe passed 1 control and failed 1 absolute-path case. Original copy-migration-absolute/relative-before.json evidence is retained; the same final probe passed both cases with separate migration-final output. Internal absolute/nested references and external aliases resolving inside the library now move to the new root. Legacy single-name references keep their meaning; genuine external paths and matching prefixes remain literal.
- **Adds SQLite table local_storage_relocation, version 1**. Only a completed copy can produce a prepared journal. One independent transaction updates directory rows and marks the journal committed, then memory selects the new root and local_path is atomically replaced as its mirror. Preparation/commit check directory snapshots and reject outer transactions, overlapping paths, duplicate identities, malformed/unknown journals and escaping cleanup plans. Copy failure preserves source and partial target. Commit acknowledgement failure checks durable state; failure of that check preserves both errors and copies, without claiming complete unknown-commit reconciliation or runtime write fencing.
- Startup selects the original library for prepared state after checking its existence and path mirror, preserving the target. Committed state selects the destination and retains the journal if mirroring or receipt cleanup fails again. A missing authoritative source/destination fails instead of creating an empty library or falling back. Startup recovery **never clears the old library**. Only a fully completed live migration attempts old-content cleanup after retained-reference/native-alias checks; failure keeps the copy.
- Receipt plans bind the intent digest, complete before/after registrations and target directory. Current row and exclusive ownership are checked before retiring copied receipts with the old registration. Only control files are removed; interruptions resume on startup and changed/shared ownership retains evidence. Legacy single-name registration snapshots remain unchanged and their receipts can still be retired by the existing recovery scan.
- Models returned by LocalManager and registered inputs share the current registration's location. List/search/detail results and covers follow repeated/round-trip moves. Removal followed by ID reuse, manager disposal and data-store replacement detach old models; no lookup in a new store silently adopts a replacement record. Already captured File objects and active reads are outside this relocation guarantee.
- Added 37 regressions; the final full suite passed **3857 tests with 2 existing skips**, LCOV **35,180/47,757 (73.66%)**. Strict analysis has zero diagnostics; formatting 6 Dart files made no changes. Python 83 tests with 3 existing skips, structure/architecture, release and Git dependency checks passed. Initial 18 brace-style diagnostics were fixed; earlier 38/23 targeted results are intermediate evidence. The final 37 additions include library-root junction and SAF path-adapter checks.
- Eight independent test VMs call exit(86) after prepared commit, first directory UPDATE before COMMIT, COMMIT, mirror rename, actual completion/intent/receipt deletion, and journal deletion. Restart checks both full database rows, 4 page contents, covers and getImages. An interrupted transaction rolls back; committed state selects the new library and always retains old payload. library-relocation-crash-v1-audit.json matches final source hashes. Flutter parent exits 79; this is not full-app, power-loss or real-SAF proof.
- Enforced business roots **173→174**. Manager/repository/migration/journal reach 140/87/96/94 project files with no registered UI or added/enlarged file SCC. Allowed edges and acyclic constraints are unchanged; foundation/JS/network cycles remain. Seven source/test/baseline paths are frozen. Original 52 items remain **24 I /27 P /1 U**; 46.2% is implementation-evidence share only.
- Previously broken absolute references with no journal still need an explicit evidence-based repair path, never basename guessing. Interrupted-copy continuation/cleanup, old-root cleanup across restart, legacy copy conflicts, complete unknown-commit reconciliation/failed detach, all writers across processes, real SAF/external races/power-loss fsync, remaining P2–P8, full CLI, declared SDK, five platforms and fixed-device performance remain open.

## P6/P7: Registered-copy cleanup receipts and retry (2026-10-07)

- Baseline `349424a`. Recovery previously skipped every registered directory before inspecting control files. Post-registration termination left both files indefinitely; failure deleting intent after completion left intent alone, without cleanup proof. Three pre-fix regressions fail for completed registered-copy cleanup, reporting mismatched registered metadata, and reporting legacy intent without completion. New receipt stages had no old equivalent and are not presented as pre-fix evidence.
- Version-1 .venera-copy-registered.json is cleanup-only, retaining the exact intent and actual local-row snapshot. Cleanup writes, flushes and reads it back before removing completion, intent and finally the receipt. Retries check ownership of remaining originals, preserving deletion errors/stacks and never deleting comic payload. Normal registration confirms the actual row before cleanup; a later explicit local-download recovery scan automatically resumes cleanup. No new background startup task was added. Database schemas/keys stay unchanged; the control-file layout gains a third reserved name.
- LocalRepository queries stored directories with parameters; LocalManager resolves existing relative/absolute references and returns every matching row. This avoids the recent-20 limit, same-title guesses and ambiguous shared directories. Metadata comparisons use SQLite millisecond precision and retain tag/chapter order. Cleanup snapshots bind ID, stored directory and metadata. Legacy completed output is fully verified in compute, then current registration and the actual native parent are rechecked before issuing a receipt. Cleanup also reuses deletion protections for retained records and actual filesystem paths, rejecting ancestor overlap and shared ownership through a Windows junction; two new cases and existing shared-directory tests cover this. Receipt-backed retries avoid rereading all payload and never register a comic or replay favorite intent.
- Receipts cannot authorize a new registration, even when both original records still exist after the local row was deleted. Shared ownership, changed identity/metadata, malformed/unknown receipts and changed original intent preserve evidence and report issues. Legacy intent without completion or a cleanup receipt cannot be acknowledged by path/title alone. Cleanup neither queries nor replays the original favorite folder; normal coordinated registration is unchanged.
- **20 new regressions** cover relative/absolute paths beyond recent results, four write/delete failure retries, original errors/stacks, identity replacement, shared directories, changed payload, same-title other directories, replay rejection with receipt alone or all three files, and malformed/unknown/mismatched receipts. An initial test substitute named delete conflicted with File's interface and was corrected. Fault-injection type queries now delegate through the original zone, matching production overrideIO's existing Dart3.11 adapter; earlier failures remain logged. Native alias protection was added after the first full run started; that run is retained as superseded evidence. Final full and five-point interruption validation use the strengthened frozen source. Expanded **270 pass**; final full suite **3820 passed, 2 existing skips**, LCOV **34,906/47,471 (73.53%)**. Strict analysis, seven-file formatting, Python83 with three existing skips, structure/architecture, version and Git-dependency checks pass.
- Five real test-VM interruption points follow registration, receipt write, completion deletion, intent deletion and receipt deletion. Each exits with86 after actual flush/deletion, then restart preserves identical local/favorite records and three pages with no new registration and no leftover control files. Checkpoint marker counts are2/3/2/1/0. copy-cleanup-crash-v2-audit.json verifies production service/SQLite behavior in Windows test VMs, not full-app, SAF, power-loss or directory-fsync acceptance.
- Enforced roots remain **173**. Metadata/record/worker/service/manager/repository reach84/6/33/143/137/87 files, without registered UI or new/enlarged SCCs; dependency constraints are unchanged and foundation/JS/network cycles remain. Seven source/test paths are frozen in copy-cleanup-final-frozen-sources.json; graph evidence is copy-cleanup-final-dependency-audit.json. Original52-item status stays **24 I / 27 P / 1 U**. Incomplete-copy resume/explicit cleanup, legacy insufficient-evidence repair, changed-record conflict resolution, cross-process ownership, SAF/external races, unknown commits/failed detach, old partial rows/orphan IDs and remaining P2–P8/CLI/declared-SDK/five-platform/fixed-device acceptance remain open.

- A separate migration audit reproduced an open defect: setNewPath copies files and updates the library path, but absolute directory values from copied imports still reference the old location. The relative-path control passes; the absolute-path case fails while complete pages remain in the destination. External copy_migration_probe.dart uses actual import/manager migration; copy-migration-before.log has one pass/one failure and two JSON snapshots retain path/page evidence. This is an unresolved P6.3 issue, not passing evidence for this batch, and takes priority next.

- Final delivery: Windows release built in 97.7s; packaged CHANGELOG/init.js/translation.json match source. app.so SHA-256 `0EB74F51BB44724B8B916A4AC851C585BD8173365AF008996715BA3C666FE86D`; all seven source/test files match final pre-suite frozen hashes. ## P6/P7: Copy ownership and recovery deduplication (2026-10-07)

- Baseline `da23617`. The old worker moved an occupied destination to *_old and copied incoming pages into the original path. A real-manager regression reproduced an existing comic reading the new pages; equal basenames in one batch also shared an output. Each distinct source now receives a separate directory without moving or reusing existing files, empty directories or comics; repeated sources copy once. LocalComic retains the original title; the generated directory name is storage identity. Existing records/directories are not migrated.
- Native storage reserves output with exclusive createTempSync and resolves source/destination-root symlinks before rejecting a destination equal to or inside its source. Two real compute isolates verify distinct output for concurrent copies. The SAF plugin has no exclusive-create or createTemp API, so its branch uses a UUID, observed-collision checks and a post-create existence check. Real providers and external races remain unverified; this is not the native exclusive-create guarantee.
- ComicDirectoryCopyResult returns per-source successes and ComicDirectoryCopyFailure diagnostics. Failure cleanup targets only acquired output, preserving original and cleanup errors/stacks; individual copy or cleanup failure does not discard other successful members. ComicImportService forwards structured failures to the existing presentation boundary. A real-isolate regression checks the error type, source path, cleanup outcome and successful count. Existing succeeded versus importedCount/issues batch semantics remain.
- Recovery already matched titles or relative directory names, but missed absolute stored paths whose directory names differ from their titles. Within original-store exclusive recovery, localDownloads reads repository directoryReferences, resolves relative directories and filters registered absolute lexical paths, retaining existing lookup and sort behavior. Absolute/relative records and a scan after a successful copy do not duplicate registration. This does not cover every path alias or parent/child overlap.
- Net **16 additional regressions**: 12 worker and four service cases cover occupied output, basenames/duplicates, real isolates, nested destinations, allocation/cleanup failures and SAF branch substitutes. SAF substitutes use real temporary files but do not validate a device provider. Occupied-output replacement and absolute-path recovery failed before the fixes. Earlier file://, query interpretation, ordering and substitute-field fixture mistakes were corrected and their logs retained. Expanded **216 tests pass**; full suite **3766 passed, 2 existing skips**; LCOV **34,517/47,041 (73.38%)**. Strict analysis is clean; five Dart files need no format changes. Python 83 tests include three existing skips; structure/architecture, version and Git-dependency checks pass.
- Enforced business roots remain **171**. The worker/service reach 32/141 project files with no registered UI and no new/enlarged SCC. Feature-edge and seven-file cycle constraints remain unchanged; the foundation/JS/network cycles remain. Five source/test paths are frozen in directory-copy-frozen-sources.json; dependency evidence is directory-copy-dependency-audit.json. Original 52-item status remains **24 I / 27 P / 1 U**.
- Durable copy ownership/recovery across process termination or failed cleanup is not implemented; manual recovery can still identify incomplete directories, and original titles/favorite intent for unregistered output cannot be reconstructed across restart. Unknown COMMIT reconciliation, failed-detach retry, old partial registrations, orphan same-ID favorites in other folders and ID reuse without a selected favorite remain open. Sync replaces favorites but not the local database, so durable intent must distinguish database replacement. Remaining P2–P8, full CLI, declared SDK, five platforms, system termination and fixed-device performance remain unaccepted.

- Final delivery checks: Windows release built in 94.9 seconds; packaged CHANGELOG/init.js/translation.json match source. app.so SHA-256 `9EA7A50CCAAFAE7A874FC10E47632AC1212765F4F4A2FED18854F0186DAFFE3D`. All five source/test paths match their hashes frozen before full validation. Flutter 3.41.6 / Dart 3.11.4 does not replace declared-SDK or five-platform acceptance. directory-copy-artifact-hashes.json binds logs, frozen records, artifacts and commit; continuation is in directory-copy-next-audit.md.

## P4/P6/P7: Coordinated local and favorite registration (2026-10-07)

- Baseline `27c1312`. Four independent test processes interrupted the old path before/after favorite insertion and after each compensation, then reopened the stores. Output retention alone did not recover registration intent: directory recovery skipped a saved local title with a missing favorite, and interrupted compensation could leave a favorite or an unregistered directory. See import-registration-crash-audit.json. This evidence led to reusing the existing SQLite coordinated-transaction conditions rather than replaying separate writes and compensations.
- local_registration_storage.dart attaches the original favorites database to the local connection. One BEGIN IMMEDIATE / COMMIT writes the local row, page-order marker and selected favorite, reusing both repositories. Only disk rollback journals are accepted; WAL/memory/off and caller-owned transactions are rejected. Schema, persisted keys and file layout are unchanged. Existing local identities and selected-folder favorite identities cannot be overwritten or counted as new success. Orphan identities in other folders and external-writer conflicts still need review.
- ComicImportService executes the transaction through the favorites mutation queue, detaches metadata before waiting, and allocates the ID at execution. Original library/admission, translated tags, ordering and timestamps remain. LocalManager publishes after commit; favorites then refreshes counts, identity indexes and follow-update notifications from committed records. The old two-step registration/compensation path is removed. Registration without favorites retains its existing single-store error classification.
- Failed writes roll back before output may be deleted. A failed COMMIT acknowledgement remains unknown; rollback and detach failures retain original causes and cleanup diagnostics. Confirmed commits retain records/pages even if detach, publication or PDF disposal fails. Runtime reconciliation of unknown acknowledgements and failed-release retry remain open; retained files are not proof of recovery.
- Four new process checkpoints follow real local INSERT, favorite INSERT, immediately before COMMIT and after COMMIT. A connection proxy calls exit(86) at those native SQL boundaries without Dart cleanup; the Flutter parent observes abnormal termination. On reopening, both databases are empty at the first three checkpoints and both retain one row after COMMIT; all output files survive. Subsequent explicit directory recovery creates only local records for aborted output and does not reconstruct favorite intent. See atomic-registration-crash-audit.json. This is Windows test-VM interruption evidence, not power-loss, full-application or five-platform acceptance.
- Net **17 additional regressions**: 17 transaction-storage tests and one queue test; four obsolete compensation combinations are replaced by three format-specific post-commit failure tests. Three-format dual-failure/PDF-disposal checks now assert coordinated rollback. Generic callback committed/unknown output protection remains. Expanded **335 tests pass**; final suite **3750 passed, 2 existing skips**; LCOV **34,476/47,003 (73.35%)**. Strict analysis is clean; seven Dart files need no format changes. Python 83 tests include three existing skips; structure/architecture, version and Git-dependency checks pass.
- Enforced business entries increase **170 → 171**. The new storage root reaches 93 project files and no registered UI, with no new/enlarged SCC. The three existing foundation/JS/network cycles and original 52-item **24 I / 27 P / 1 U** status remain. Eight source/test/baseline paths are frozen. Old-version partial registrations, durable intent/conflict repair for unregistered output, copy failures, complete lifecycle and remaining boundaries/settings/source/compatibility plus CLI/declared SDK/five-platform/fixed-device performance remain open.

## P4/P6/P7: Import registration state and output retention (2026-10-07)

- Baseline `c6335e2`. Real SQLite favorite INSERT and local DELETE rejection triggers reproduced the defect through PDF, EPUB and CBZ conversion: the local row remained while pages/cover were deleted, and compensation masked the original favorite error. All three regressions failed before the fix; see import-compensation-before.log.
- ComicImportService uses the existing PersistenceFailure contract. Validation failures and ordinary local-write failures with verified absent records report notCommitted; post-write errors, failed verification and uncertain SQLite rollback remain unknown. Favorite failure triggers independent local/favorite compensation so one failure cannot prevent the other attempt. Original causes/stacks and all compensation/verification diagnostics survive. Successful IDs, favorite identity, notifications and data admission are unchanged; no cross-database transaction was added.
- ComicImportOutput is used by DocumentImportSession and CBZ to track output ownership. Conversion errors before registration and explicit notCommitted failures permit deletion; committed, unknown and unclassified callback failures retain output. A registrar must explicitly confirm non-commit to discard files on failure. Existing rejection fixtures now declare that contract and still verify cleanup/retry. A throwing Future alone is no longer evidence that no database write occurred.
- Output-deletion failures preserve the original error and cleanup diagnostics. PDF document disposal is awaited once after success/failure or rejection before conversion. Release errors cannot replace registration/compensation causes; a release failure after successful registration reports committed. PDF pages and release failures use injected test doubles, not evidence for every native-plugin/device failure. Cache workspaces retain their existing cleanup policy.

## P5/P8: Original chapter loading snapshots and reader-shell callback ownership (2026-10-07)

- Baseline `caeef27`. ReaderChapterRequest captures comic/type, local manager, actual online source and ordered chapters. Its immutable grouped copy preserves empty groups and repeated IDs across groups. Admission and complete-Future completion validate the original target; retired delivery is cancelled while original read failures remain owned by ImageWork. Gallery/main loading and waterfall loading share this request, with ownership checks around migration and reading-mode preparation.
- WaterfallController captures chapter ID before reading instead of resolving a mutable map after await. Continuous views retain original loader/ID/error callbacks. Replacing image work, navigation, comic, source key, chapter count or cross-chapter mode retires the old waterfall/preload and releases its loading notification. Original accepted source calls still drain fully; stale results/frames cannot change replacement content.
- ReaderImagesHost observes source-manager publications and replaces the loading attempt when content identity changes. Title-only changes update presentation/interaction validity; local-comic comment-source changes preserve the image viewport. Main loading retains its original chapter number. Shell actions bind the original shell, route and actual viewport, rejecting obsolete interaction after unmount, mode/content replacement or exit holds without late ancestor lookups.
- Adds thirteen regressions: three original waterfall IDs, five real-reader cases (metadata, changed-ID/unmounted admission, retired shell callbacks, actual source replacement with exit draining), four immutable grouped snapshot/admission/success-or-failure drain cases and one same-ImageWork comic replacement. Seven old-implementation regressions initially failed and then passed. ## P4/P5/P8: Context-menu route ownership and adaptive layout (2026-10-07)

- Baseline `d5b79fb`. MenuRouteController retains each pending/open popup, its original root navigator and parent route chain; both admission and deferred insertion validate the original owner. Closing retires synchronously and removes only that route in a microtask. Old handles, duplicate selections and covering pages cannot affect replacement routes. Selection retires before pop and rejects actions if pop observers close/dispose the owner. Completion detaches target listeners and clears route references without accumulating parent-route completion callbacks.

State owners retire menus on deactivate/dispose; comic cards and search rows use local ContextMenuRegion ownership, distinguishing stable-target rebuilds from replacement. Details and favorite-image menus capture their original target. - Gesture requests distinguish target validity beneath their own popup from current-route admission for actions. Menus observe the original reader, source manager and close/page notifications. Chapter, viewport, preference, source, exit-hold and unmount changes retire the original popup; accepted image reads remain owned and drained by the original ImageWork.
- Layout uses actual content size within safe-area/keyboard bounds; empty entries do not push. Labels wrap, tall menus scroll and rows remain at least 48px, with icons aligned to the first line. ## P2/P4/P5: Gesture targets and pointer lifetimes (2026-10-07)

- Baseline `36d9579`. ReaderGestureDetector no longer owns ReaderState or reads context.reader; ReaderGestureHost composes ReaderGestureRequest and original-shell actions at the UI boundary. A second finger, replacement, cancellation or removal ends zoom on the original viewport and cancels favorite dragging without collecting. During a build, pause release remains immediate while only the ancestor UI refresh is coalesced after the frame, fixing mode-switch setState-during-build failures.

## P4/P5: scrollbar viewport replacement and chapter reply route ownership (2026-10-07)

- Baseline `8b145dc`. AppScrollBar detaches a replaced borrowed controller and observes its replacement without disposing caller-owned objects. A drag retains the original ScrollPosition and is retired on controller/position replacement or unmount. Movement uses current pixels and bounds. Layout/content metric notifications synchronize the actual viewport after the frame; missing, multiple, unready or non-finite positions and too-short tracks expose no invalid thumb.
- Each chapter comment view owns its reply route through ReaderSidebarBinding. Request replacement, unmount and original-target invalidation remove only that route; nested replies release recursively without dismissing unrelated covering pages. Removing presentation does not finish accepted sends/reads early: the original ImageWork still waits for real completion.
- ReaderState comment requests expose detachable subscriptions to the original reader and source manager. Reader changes, freeze, disposal and source publication retire old requests; a source replacement also rebuilds embedded comments. ComicSourceManager.current only reads an already assembled, non-closing instance and never initializes it. The comments preference, title and manager identity participate in validity checks; subscriptions never migrate to a later manager.
- Sidebar admission and actual route push check the original request and parent route. Covered old pages cannot open new sidebars, while immediate replacement remains possible before a retired owned route is removed. Replies retain 500px width, no barrier, existing safe-area/back behavior and use zero-duration route transitions with reduced motion.

## P2/P4/P5: acknowledged native volume ownership (2026-10-06)

The Dart controller consumes errors only in its serial tail; operation callers retain the real failure, and leases disappear only after successful release.
- ReaderState registers volume separately with its original application/window before initialization work. Removed or migrated pages retain pending native ownership. Failed release blocks core shutdown; retry does not replay ReaderSession progress, duration or notification. Current route, foreground state, exit holds and settings govern input admission. Covered/background/closing pages release volume, and reversible shutdown failure restores subscription only when still eligible.

## P2/P3/P4: original host ownership for standalone setting saves (2026-10-06)

- Baseline `dbaefb8`. SettingsSaveState captures its original window, SelectionTasksScope and optional SettingsSaveScope in initState. Each save registers its complete Future before the persist callback. Original window/application owners keep waiting after page removal, retaining original errors/stacks. Repeated close waits for the original result without replaying login, web-open or other business callbacks. Closing hosts reject new writes, including reentrant close during registration.
- Changing the window, application registry or reading session changes the current owner while accepted saves/repair chains remain with the old host. Same-key edits on the new page cannot acknowledge or retry old operations. An already accepted successful old-host assignment can still repair its own predecessors without delivering onSaved to the new page. Final application close also suppresses that callback; reversible window preparation retains existing save behavior.
- A latest successful assignment remains while predecessors are pending. A late predecessor failure retains the exit constraint; explicit retry uses the latest value instead of replaying the old value, acknowledging only corresponding errors that already exist. Caller keys distinguish targets/fields, and stale isCurrent targets skip retry. ## P2/P4/P5: original application ownership of reading sessions (2026-10-06)

- Baseline `7708412`. ReaderState registers its original SelectionTasksScope and window before initial read-later/image work. History persistence captures the original HistoryManager and duration target. Application close waits for progress, duration, notification and shared ImageWork image/layout/settings writes after page removal. Replacing the host/window closes the original session without moving pending registrations. A closed host refuses initialization work and notifications from a new session.

- ImageWork.retainTasks registers outer owners before returning work. Successful tasks detach promptly; reported failures retain independent ImageWorkTask close results until their matching repair acknowledgement. Detaching the observer does not remove accepted registrations; reentrant cancellation or failed registration refuses actual work. The reader session and shared tasks register separately, so a page drain cannot erase a failure before core close.

Ownership is removed only after release succeeds; HTTP/source-transaction close contracts remain unchanged.

## P2/P4/P6/P7: one-shot selection ownership by window and application host (2026-10-06)

- Baseline `fe906ed`. foundation/selection_operation.dart owns the complete picker/consumer/release Future. ApplicationHost owns SelectionTaskRegistry and all platforms receive its Scope; removed old windows or pages without a desktop window retain their tasks. Failure blocks core producer/store closure; retry never replays import.
- SettingsTaskPresenter, favorite JSON/create and ordinary local-comic entrypoints use complete tasks. Importer selection methods explicitly receive SelectionOperation. Headless ImportComicPresentation no longer resolves a global root navigator; interactive callers bind their task. Source preview forwards its existing scope check to native selection. ## P3/P4/P6/P7: durable source script, settings and data transactions (2026-10-06)

- Baseline `fa8ec16`. New enforced entry source_transaction_journal.dart owns `.source-transactions/transactions.sqlite` under the canonical data root: UUID, root, phase, script/settings/data before values, every after snapshot, owned staging and digest. SQLite FULL/secure_delete, a persistent owner.lock and in-process ownership coordinate access. Installation records before its first script write; replacement records after initialization but before changing the live script; removal records before settings persist. Existing preparation/resource queues remain in use without upgrading to exclusive admission.
- Appdata.beforePersist synchronously records immutable output at the actual writer queue head, before memory publication or either settings-file write; async hooks are rejected. Recovery limits settings edits to this source's page fields/origin key and uses each primary/backup's actual bytes and field presence, respecting syncdata filtering. Registered memory before/after values also recognize live rollback defaults and copies of published settings into backups. Unrelated fields, other origins and unknown updates survive; invalid prior documents allow only exact-snapshot restoration.
- Each physical staged-data write first appends its snapshot and enters applying. Preparing/rollback restore old state, applying completes the latest registered snapshot, and committed only cleans evidence. Failure before any data applies records rollback before runtime/script/settings recovery. Failure after data applies retains the new source and existing save-retry semantics. Failed durable commit decisions retain evidence and freeze writes; notification/native-release errors do not roll back the script.
- Recovery attempts resources independently. It records recovery staging before writing, flushes complete bytes before rename, seals the result as committed, then removes known staging/empty directories and acknowledges the row. Conflicts do not block independent restoration; unknown siblings, modified staging and legacy version 1 script-* directories are retained. Startup runs import recovery → source transactions → ordinary source-data staging → appdata initialization. Imports/exports can only clean committed evidence; pending effects block transfer so initialized memory cannot become stale after disk recovery.
- Regressions cover real manager script intent before installation init, every data snapshot, rollback and omitted legacy metadata; real bootstrap restores a bad script through import rollback then removes it through source recovery, while three corrupt journals block appdata initialization. Transfer guards, queue/hooks, omitted/null fields, sync filtering, backups, later edits and conflicts are covered. Fifteen independent Dart VM termination cases cover install/replace/remove and interrupted recovery, including active process locking. These are disk-protocol process tests, not full Flutter manager process-termination acceptance.

Cleanup does not replay edits, write live data or publish notifications. Later successful saves attempt older cleanup; close retries cleanup before native references are released. Every receipt keeps its original directory.
- Successful cleanup clears only its matching diagnostic and the same already-applied write's storage failure. Uncommitted writes still need saving; publication failures still need publication. External conflicts and adapters without ownership receipts remain visible even after a later successful save. A newly failing write keeps its original result without an immediate retry; final close remains shared and cannot reopen the instance.
- Runtime recovery and real QuickJS manager-close regressions cover later live data, active same-key writes, missing/changed journals, freeze/admission, original directories, uncommitted saves, publication and unowned adapters. ## P3/P4/P6/P7: durable ownership and recovery of source-data staging (2026-10-06)

- Baseline `ad8337a`. New controlled business entry source_data_journal.dart records UUID, canonical data root, source key, intended contents and SHA-256 in SQLite with FULL durability before staging-directory creation. Cleanup and acknowledgement are separate; deleted rows use secure_delete. Ordinary/staged saves share this storage path; recovery logs are excluded from library snapshot exports.
- A short directory gate coordinates intent creation/orphan-lock cleanup. Each write holds its own cross-process lock and an in-process registration; independent writes retain separate staging directories. Recovery rejects live holders and retains failed cleanup records. Gate conflicts also release the original operation descriptor so recovery can be retried after repair.
- Recovery deletes only contents matching the intended bytes or their prefix, followed by a non-recursive empty-directory removal. External edits, unknown siblings, invalid ownership/root records and aliased paths remain with diagnostics. Recovery never overwrites the live .data file or later saves. Empty operation locks left after acknowledgement can be reclaimed; legacy unrecorded staging remains untouched.
- Core startup recovers imports first, then source staging before settings/database initialization. Import also recovers staging before replacing resources. Real bootstrap tests restore a source directory containing old residue before cleaning it; corrupt import/source journals independently prevent early settings/database open.
- Regressions cover ownership, later writes, external conflicts, unknown files, Windows junctions, gate-failure descriptor release and repeated recovery. Separate processes are killed after intent/partial write/before replacement/after replacement, with live-owner rejection. The three existing staging windows additionally kill the cleanup process and resume again. Expanded coverage passed 378 tests; a later preparation-failure refinement initially classified preflight errors as unfinished cleanup, failing two existing save tests. It now retains that intent only after directory preparation actually starts; failure logs remain. Frozen verification follows.
- This is a source-data staging-cleanup protocol, not the script/config/data transaction for installation/replacement/removal. Legacy residue review, coordinated retries of already-reported runtime failures, full source cancellation, durable dirty/revision/business-content baselines, final core close, P5/P7/P8 and full CLI/five-platform/fixed-device performance remain open. ## P3/P4/P7: source instance identity and late capability results (2026-10-06)

- Baseline `8b33252`. JS instances carry WeakMap identities. Each parse creates an independent identifier; lexical sendMessage and all five SDK data methods carry that identity. Dart receivers validate both the actual JsEngine and identifier against the registered source. Replacing/removing/reloading the same key cannot redirect old calls to new data. Unowned global data messages are rejected; other protocols are unchanged. API docs describe the contract.
- SourceConstructionReads provides only the previous runtime's deep data snapshot and setting defaults while parsing. First construction without a previous runtime keeps absent reads, then loads disk data. The scope releases after parse; writes wait until registration rather than borrowing the old instance. Rollback restores the previous JS/Dart identity; retained callbacks of the failed new instance remain invalid.
- SourceParserContext captures engine/identity; all nine capability parsers stop dynamically executing ComicSource.sources.key. Calls and result delivery check the instance, rejecting and releasing undelivered native graphs; toCompletion retains actual Promise waiting and final release. Retained settings/dynamic-category callbacks, dynamic settings and source init use the same context. Delivered image-config native functions remain owned by their image task and may continue pure processing; their source data calls still validate identity. This is not a sandbox against arbitrary script edits to the global registry.
- Login captures its Dart source before awaiting and waits for the source draft save after JS success. Save failure returns an error and retains memory credentials for persistence retry. Late login cannot select the replacement. Favorites and both comment relogin flows capture the same instance and recheck after awaits rather than logging in or resending against a new source.

## P3/P4/P6/P7: atomic source files and staged-write handoff (2026-10-06)

- Baseline `fc4f88b`. SourceDataStorage uses an injected SourceDataFiles dependency for both ordinary and staged persistence. A unique directory beside the target owns the complete flushed file and rename replacement; the old target is never truncated or deleted first. Cleanup removes only that file and the empty directory, retaining write/cleanup errors, applied state and the residue path. Manager/parser pass the instance dependency to sources; a live manager rejects a different storage dependency.
- Staged commits acquire global admission before the file queue and are included in close. Revisions drain snapshots accepted during write/cleanup before synchronously ending staging. Staged saveData acknowledges a captured draft, not persistence. After any snapshot commits, later failures and explicit retries retain applied state. Ordinary saves can persist current data; explicit commit retries keep the accepted snapshot, and a newer successful save prevents an older retry from restoring stale contents.
- Applied writes attempt publication even after cleanup failure, preserving both errors. Later successful writes do not erase unresolved cleanup diagnostics, which close also reports. Manager failures retain nested data recovery paths and never roll back a script over committed credentials. Replacement captures the frozen original memory before init and persists it even when init never saves.
- Fourteen new regressions cover six save/draft/cleanup/close cases, three real QuickJS installation/replacement cases, two storage failure/concurrency cases and three deterministic process termination boundaries. Partial write, flushed-before-rename and after-rename windows retain complete old/new target JSON; the next write leaves previous process residue untouched. Two initial legacy-fixture cleanup/lint mistakes were fixed and their logs retained. Expanded targeted tests passed 290 cases; additional process/save tests passed 24. ## P3/P4/P6/P7: Source data snapshots and reversible write freezing (2026-10-06)

- Baseline `bfa92ae`. loadData/saveData acquire global admission before local file work. Each ordinary save captures its directory and JSON content with an independent Future; pending saves are no longer shared across admission. Later unsaved edits cannot enter an earlier request. Bound instances reject new saves into another directory, while accepted requests finish in their original directory. Reads join close ownership and recheck epoch/instance before late publication.
- File queues exclude notification. Ordinary access releases before ordered publication, allowing an ordinary save's notification to await fresh exclusive work when no outer data operation is held. Callers inside preparation/exclusive scope must still avoid self-dependent notification waits. The local publication sequence only protects notification flags; it is not durable sync revision. Close drains all accepted reads/saves/notifications; original file errors remain observable and a later successful file write clears the retained file failure. Notification errors carry applied state. Managers with sources/closing tasks reject close waits inside data operations to avoid awaiting their own later saves.
- SourceDataWriteFreeze blocks new edits/saves and drains admitted file work. Not-yet-admitted requests become invalid by epoch rather than being awaited inside exclusive work. Failed writes still settle the barrier, retaining errors for callers/final close; draining is not proof of persistence. Only replacement copies JSON; removal/reload avoid unnecessary whole-data copies. Replacement captures at freeze time, recovery reopens the original instance, preserved runtime instances reopen after reload, and retired instances close.
- Reload independently attempts JS restoration, reference release and each source's write resumption; cleanup/publication errors after reload report applied. The new failed-reload case exposed Windows separator mismatches in existing-script recognition; path.equals now preserves rollback instead of silently skipping a failed existing script. JS save/delete use editDataSync to validate admission, ownership and JSON drafts before memory edits while retaining synchronous APIs.
- Thirteen new real-file/SQLite/QuickJS regressions cover captured path/deep content, exclusive saves, notification admission, queued close, stale reads/writes, sync rejection, staged snapshots, replacement/reload recovery and real archive import. Earlier coalescing tests now verify independent snapshots/publications; two Fakes implement the new binding contract. Initial Fake/path failures remain logged. Expanded coverage passed 291 cases; added boundaries/path repair are included in frozen validation below.
- Entries remain 108; dependencies unchanged. Public mutable data/UI/account writers, old key-based JS and Dart capability callbacks, atomic ordinary-file replacement/durable recovery, derived writes during staged commit, durable dirty/revision/content baselines, core shutdown and P5/P7/P8/full CLI/five-platform/fixed-device performance remain open. All 52 statuses stay 23 I / 28 P / 1 U; this does not complete every source boundary, crash recovery or the optimization plan.

## P3/P4/P6/P7: Source preparation and whole-data operation ordering (2026-10-06)

- Baseline `04011cb`. External `source_admission_deadlock_repro_test.dart` reproduced the cycle using real QuickJS, installation and exclusive reload: init settled while configuration/reload blocked; the bounded exclusive timeout released both and cleanup drained. It reproduces import acquisition order, not a complete archive import. Logs/hashes: `source-admission-deadlock-repro*`.
- AppDataOperations adds prepare in the same queue, serializing preparation/exclusive work without holding ordinary access across preparation. Async/sync accesses may pass a not-yet-started exclusive operation while preparing, including native callbacks without the initiating Dart Zone. Existing FIFO remains otherwise. Access cannot upgrade to preparation/exclusive, preparation cannot upgrade to exclusive; descendants, empty-drain sealing, expiry and failure release remain owned.
- Manager mutations acquire preparation before their local queue; import-owned reload does not wait on later external requests. Initialization acquires admission before starting Init and shares only within the same live scope. Init.init no longer starts through a virtual waiting guard. Unstarted ensureInit within admission fails explicitly; ordinary waiters still do not start work. Close tracks all accepted requests including queued preparation, rejecting self-dependent close inside data operations.
- Background batches initialize concurrently under separate preparation without extending startup across network waits. Admission rechecks instances and skips retired objects; import-loaded sources initialize after exclusive release. A 15-second diagnostic timeout does not release an unfinished actual Promise. Registry/data publication clears data/preparation scope so resulting work acquires independently.

- Ready/failed initialization reuses its existing result without preparation, preventing repeated init from waiting on background Promises. The concurrent-source case also verifies repeated init/ensureInit return immediately. ## P3/P4/P6/P7: Source configuration mutations and failure recovery (2026-10-05)

- Baseline `cd33dda`. SourceConfigurationChange saves pages and one origin in the same appdata draft, capturing old values at the queue head. Before initialization it captures only expected origin identity, not whole page lists. Recovery restores fields still equal to this mutation's publication, preserves unrelated origin keys and newer fields, and reports conflicts together with persistence failures.
- Install/replace/remove collect stage errors and stacks, preserving the original error type after successful recovery. Incomplete recovery uses SourceMutationFailure without skipping later recovery stages. Parser rollback independently attempts JS restoration, callback disposal and rollback-reference release; parsing and temporary-file cleanup preserve earlier failures.
- SourceScriptCheckpoint saves original bytes and a hash manifest before mutation, checking file contents before replacement and final commit. Recovery atomically replaces through a temporary file only while contents still match this operation, preserving external edits and reporting conflicts. Failed recovery retains its `source_recovery` backup path. This is diagnostic backup for in-process recovery, not a complete cross-process settings/data/JS journal or protection from external replacement after the content check.
- Staged data file commit is separate from notification. Notification, old-callback release and post-commit cleanup failures report applied and keep new script/configuration/credentials. Close still waits for accepted writes and retains write/publication errors. Install/replace timeout retains the staged source until the actual init Promise settles before rollback, protecting the old key-based data bridge from late saves. Background startup retains independent initialization and close waiting.
- Uninstall persists page/origin cleanup before removing the script and JS/Dart registrations, independently recovering failures. Successful removal releases callbacks and retains `.data`. UI `_validatePages` direct edits/unawaited persistence are removed; search origins are pruned too. Retired objects cannot replace/uninstall a later instance at the same path. Confirmation layout is unchanged; complete UI/window task ownership remains tracked.

## P2/P3/P4/P6: Source repository configuration and save retries (2026-10-05)

- Baseline `c15fbce`. Migration awaits appdata initialization before acquiring global access and sharing migration work. Drafts read current configuration; partial persistence retains published values and retries writes. Repository/origin edits merge at the settings queue head. Notifications do not lend storage admission; removal, linking and unlinking validate their original targets and preserve unrelated queued edits.
- `SourceRepositorySave` captures the creation ID, input, original repository and data directory. Successful catalog validation is reused; failed validation can retry. Actual saves recheck duplicate URLs and target identity. Retries neither create duplicates, overwrite later edits nor recreate deleted repositories. Network validation holds no ordinary data admission, and save Futures are not shared across admission contexts.
- Editor/removal/link/unlink dialogs use SettingsSaveState for persistence, retry, back waits and original-window handoff. Import captures preview, selection, file reader and installation queue. Existing repositories still finish persistence; dispatch requires successful saving and a valid route/navigation admission. ## P6/P3/P4: Appdata draft edits and shared settings persistence (2026-10-05)

- Baseline: `e056652`. `updateSettings` and `updateImplicit` acquire global access before the local queue, retain the admitted directory and edit the latest queued state. Synchronous callbacks use detached drafts; JSON and excluded-field validation finish before publication. Retained draft references cannot mutate published values later. Accidental async callbacks are rejected and their eventual errors observed; callbacks are not authorized to perform unrelated side effects.
- Only changed fields are replaced, preserving unchanged legacy collection identities. This avoids detaching unrelated existing writers but does not protect every public mutable collection or synchronous setter. Persistence can partially commit, so real write failure retains applied memory and reports the original error. Nested edits and setter notifications publish together after I/O, without lending admission to listeners; sync requests follow successful persistence.
- Legacy save methods acquire admission and serialize at the head of the queue, after earlier edits. Both appdata file contents are prepared before any I/O and both real writes are awaited. The queue registers its tail before invoking callbacks and drops completed idle tails, preserving error ownership without scheduling new work through a retired caller Zone. Explicit initialization starts its Init attempt after admission; import, checkpoint restoration and recovery loading share the queue. Formats and fallback-file rules remain unchanged.
- Shared settings controls retain immutable field descriptors and use the existing typed stores against the supplied draft. Unused PreferenceBinding and bind forwarding APIs are removed. Switches/selectors await persistence; sliders preview the latest input while saving each edit in order. Only the latest success for the current field invokes its callback, and late menus check the field and available options. Controls register their real save with the window and transfer unfinished work on forced removal. Page ordering saves at each edit, waits on back/exit and offers retry, instead of starting a write in dispose.
- Search history returns Futures and serializes deduplication/deletion/clearing after admission. Callers await results or explicitly handle background errors, and menus retain keywords instead of late list indices. Shortcut additions merge against the current draft and success feedback awaits persistence. Sync version/time updates use drafts and return the allocated version; the update-check timestamp is rechecked and persisted after admission, with background errors logged.
