## P6/P4: Ordinary favorites admission and awaited callers (2026-10-05)

- Baseline: `ae4deb0`. Ordinary mutations acquire AppDataOperations before joining the owner's queue. Comics, tags, destinations and ordering inputs are copied before queuing. Asynchronous queries and identity refreshes share the local queue, waiting for real read-only isolate completion before later mutations. Exclusive import/export/close drains accepted work. Initialization now includes identity publication; synchronous close can still invalidate unfinished work.
- Folder deletion/rename updates related settings together and awaits persistence. A post-commit tracking observer failure still publishes ordinary views and returns PersistenceFailure with committed state. Multi-destination moves copy all destinations and remove the source in one outer transaction. Follow-update metadata and times commit together; an unchanged timestamp preserves an existing unread flag.
- Follow-update batches, metadata refresh and network import retain their source connection generation. Cancellation is checked before SQL after admission; retired source results cannot write into a reopened database. Import receipts retain owner and generation. Publication is admitted asynchronously; stale receipts do not publish after reopen, whose initialization rebuilds current caches. A forcibly removed dialog still awaits publication of committed results, and publication failure never repeats SQL.
- Favorite callers add waiting, error feedback, input snapshots and route checks. Query generations reject late lists and missing folders are not queried. The asynchronous confirmation dialog suppresses duplicate submission and acknowledges committed failures without replaying SQL. Reordering registers each save immediately; back/window exit joins the latest save and forced removal transfers it to the window. The reader's existing 200ms favorite update belongs to ImageWork: work cancelled before SQL does not start, while accepted writes finish before exit.
- Local storage import/exclusive operations acquire global access before local ownership, including direct comic registration. This avoids a replacement/import/favorite-admission wait cycle. Settings notifications and post-save sync requests do not borrow data-operation permission; this does not complete all appdata memory edits and persistence.

## P6/P7: Cookie admission and response ownership (2026-10-05)

- Baseline: `59f6d35`. Cookie open/schema work, reads, writes, deletions, expiration cleanup during request reads and close use the same AppDataOperations. Synchronous bridges cannot wait for exclusive work: `accessSync` rejects before executing when replacement is active or queued. Internal calls reuse live permission; publication cannot borrow it; explicitly registered asynchronous descendants remain owned until completion. Existing synchronous APIs are not changed into promises.
- Dio captures the cookie connection after admission and holds access only for SQL, not the network round trip. Responses acquire access again and check connection identity. Old Set-Cookie values are discarded after same-path reopening or replacement; the HTTP response still returns. A response queued behind export saves normally when its owner is unchanged. Cancellation while queued starts neither SQL nor transport. SQL failures return DioException with the original cause/stack instead of being logged and ignored.
- Real QuickJS regressions confirm synchronous arrays from Network.getCookies and existing undefined returns/ordering from set/delete. Missing stores and replacement conflicts throw explicitly instead of masquerading as successful writes or empty reads. The incorrect Promise annotation in assets/init.js is corrected; the bridge-only void-async clearCookies forwarding method is removed.
- Web login and Cloudflare capture their original connection before opening the flow. Saves copy cookies/lists before waiting for admission, reject retired connections, and finish before the success branch. A capture failure after login-page unmount is logged without touching its UI. A newly exposed narrow-dialog overflow in the password-disabled message is fixed. This does not establish complete login/source-memory transactions, route ownership or WebView platform lifecycle acceptance.

## P6: History write admission and replacement coordination (2026-10-05)

- Baseline: `5a27069`. `AppDataOperations` coordinates concurrent ordinary access and exclusive import/export in FIFO order. Waiting exclusive work prevents later access from overtaking it. Internal work can reenter, while ordinary access cannot upgrade to exclusivity. Owners wait for registered descendants; empty drains revoke capabilities before yielding. Queued actions retain their caller Zone and independent coordinator identity.
- History initialization, progress, duration, imports, metadata and deletions acquire global access before joining the local queue. Paths and generations are captured at admission. Replacement waits for earlier writes and actual isolate work; later edits capture the reopened connection. Writes already accepted before direct close still finish against their original database without publishing into a later generation. Metadata also checks its original generation immediately before SQL.
- History notifications and external-import commit callbacks clear the data-operation capability. Listener work acquires its own queue position. Initialization callers share actual work and the completion Future; an exclusive importer can start the same initialization already queued by an external caller rather than waiting for its own release. ## P6/P7: Durable upload journal and remote content reconciliation (2026-10-05)

- This unit starts from `69ed2ee`. A separate DELETE/FULL `.data-sync-upload.sqlite` persists the operation UUID, original endpoint fingerprint, version, epoch-day filename, complete SHA-256/length, retention candidates and finalization state. Production exports snapshot and fixed export-staging directly into the owned application-data operation directory. Recovery only cleans acquired paths; unexpected entries or links block cleanup.
- Initial upload and recovery use the same pure Dart executor. Prepared/putPending writes must succeed before PUT. Restart never re-exports or increments the version. A complete matching GET confirms the upload; only an authoritative 404 permits create-only `If-None-Match: *` with the same name and bytes. A 412 triggers another probe. Authentication, server, partial-response and transport errors are not absence; different content keeps recovery evidence and is never overwritten.
- Global retention remains the first archive for that day and, for an original listing of at least ten entries, the oldest archive under the existing comparator. Candidates are deduplicated and exclude the new archive. Content is checked before deletion with the current strong `If-Match` ETag. Changed content is preserved; missing strong validators explicitly leave post-commit cleanup pending. An archive deleted after confirmation is never recreated.
- Once confirmed, only retention, the fixed observed commit time, actual native connection close and local cleanup remain. Diagnostics retain all stages. The controller writes v3 upload intent, reconstructs recovery against the original endpoint, validates a terminal receipt, durably clears its marker, then acknowledges the journal. Restart conservatively retains pending edits. Legacy v1/v2 upload or corrupt evidence remains blocked; configuration recovery follows the resolved commit state and original checkpoint.
- The remote port is Flutter-free. Production GET hashes the complete stream and joins native completion; verified immutable upload bytes survive Basic/Digest retries. Digest signs the actual encoded target and query. GET/PUT/DELETE and SDK PROPFIND reject endpoint redirects; RHttpAdapter now maps each request's followRedirects/maxRedirects to native settings.
- A parent kills separate Dart VMs at production journal/executor checkpoints. A local HTTP peer covers stored PUT/DELETE with lost acknowledgement, missing-object continuation, 412 races, probe errors and damaged snapshots. Separate HTTP regressions compose the real controller, transfer and WebDAV remote. These do not claim full Flutter executable termination or power-loss validation. Final frozen verification is recorded at the end of this section.
- Format boundaries: the new upload journal has schema version 1 and controller upload records use v3. Existing application databases and settings keys are not migrated. Legacy v1/v2 operation samples remain readable and conservatively block uploads. Archive names retain epoch day and second-field version parsing for existing downloads. Older code cannot recover v3; finish unresolved recovery in this version before downgrading, preserving journals rather than treating code rollback as data rollback.
- All-writer admission, the dirty-marker gap and content baseline, concurrent edits during import, remaining backup/native lifecycle work, complete CLI, five-platform and fixed-device performance acceptance remain open. The original 52 items remain 23 I / 28 P / 1 U. Three new business entries bring enforcement to 102.

- Final frozen verification: **2605 Windows Flutter tests pass with 2 existing host skips**; LCOV covers **24,139/38,714 lines (62.35%)**. Strict analysis has zero diagnostics. Formatting for 25 Dart files, diff checks, Python 74 tests/3 existing skips, structure/architecture (102 entries), Git-dependency and version gates pass. Focused suites include 22 real-process cases, 20 executor tests including three SQLite trigger write failures, 38 remote tests, 3 production HTTP integrations, 175 compatibility tests, 4 export tests and 8 native redirect tests. They overlap with the full suite and are not added together. The final retention-threshold regression preserves the original filtered listing length; before/fixed logs are retained and the full suite was rerun afterwards.

## P6: Durable import journal and startup recovery (2026-10-05)

- This unit starts from `2ec3541`. `AppDataImportJournal` uses a separate SQLite journal under the application data directory with DELETE/FULL. Before replacement it persists a fixed resource allowlist, existence and content hashes, and completes immutable backups. Database groups include main/journal/WAL/SHM; metadata includes appdata/syncdata primary/.bak/.tmp files; the source directory is recorded independently.
- Each mutation follows a durable changing record. Uncommitted recovery repeatedly copies immutable backups, surviving interruption of recovery itself. Database restoration isolates this import's sidecars before restoring and reopening the complete group. Applied/rolled-back records only clean owned directories and never revisit live files; old terminal cleanup does not block another import. Missing manifest rows, invalid hashes and filesystem aliases are rejected. Destination-parent validation does not traverse unrelated comic directories.
- `createCoreBootstrap` recovers after environment paths are ready but before appdata reads or any database opens. Uncommitted records restore the old snapshot; committed records only clean. Invalid journals prevent partially replaced data from opening. Journal-read errors retain the original and recovery diagnostics with `recoveryRequired`, rather than becoming not-applied and clearing sync evidence.
- Version 2 sync operations carry the import identity and previous configuration checkpoint. The production controller reads matching terminal download receipts through a narrow port and resumes fixed commit timestamps, publication and configuration persistence. It durably clears the sync marker before acknowledging the import receipt. Marker/ack failures remain retryable; orphan terminal receipts only clean owned directories. Version 1, malformed or unmatched evidence still blocks blind replay. Without a durable content baseline, restarted recovery conservatively preserves pending changes rather than claiming local content is unchanged.
- Original recovery reads and acknowledgements join exit preparation, task waiting and persistence drain. Late reads while disposed or held for exit do not begin configuration restoration or new acknowledgement. Accepted acknowledgements retain and await their original Future and errors.
- Focused and final full-suite evidence is appended below. Actual Dart VM child processes are killed by their parent across preparation, target removal, partial database/directory/metadata replacement, commit, repeated rollback/cleanup interruption and receipt acknowledgement. Separate real Flutter importer, recreated-controller and bootstrap tests verify application wiring; pure journal subprocess tests are not claimed as full Flutter executable termination tests.
- All-writer admission, the data-write-to-dirty window and content baselines, remote upload journaling/conditional writes/content reconciliation, comic-backup import/cleanup and remaining lifecycle/architecture/platform/performance work are still outstanding. This adds durable recovery and production wiring without claiming concurrent-edit protection, power-loss atomicity or complete P6 acceptance. The original 52 items remain 23 I / 28 P / 1 U.

## P6/P7: Sync commit states and in-process finalization recovery (2026-10-05)

- This unit starts from committed `0d552c9`. The preceding 2369 Windows Flutter passes, 2 host skips, 60.50% LCOV and successful Windows release remain historical evidence. Final frozen-source validation passes 2429 Windows Flutter tests with 2 existing host skips, LCOV of 22,772/37,284 lines (61.08%), and a successful Windows release build.
- Sync distinguishes `notApplied`, `applied` and `recoveryRequired`; structured failures retain every stage's original error, stack and recovery path. A remote write that started without a confirmed outcome remains uncertain rather than becoming a safe upload retry. Notification, timestamp or resource-cleanup failure does not retract a confirmed applied state.
- Application-data import awaits actual `saveData` completion and persists an exact settings/search-history checkpoint in the operation's backup directory before replacement. Rollback attempts each resource independently, retaining backups and diagnostics when recovery is incomplete. Cleanup failure after application retains an in-process continuation that retries only this import directory's cleanup, without another import. Failure of both application-data file saves preserves both causes and stacks.
- Transfer retains the outcomes of import, publication, sync-time persistence, connection close and file cleanup. In-process retry completes only unfinished follow-up steps and does not repeat successful steps. The controller suppresses only explicit publication of this import's notification, rather than treating the entire download wait as an import-notification interval. Existing real remote-close and cancellation waits remain in effect.
- Real application subscriptions exposed false pending changes from source reload and favorite-identity cache initialization during import/rollback. The synchronous publisher now passes through transfer, participant and importer to each notification, including favorite-cache publication after its asynchronous query completes. Independent CRUD, cache refreshes and source edits keep ordinary notifications. Six real runtime-factory/SQLite/JS/local-ZIP success, rollback and concurrent-edit cases pass, along with 85 combined tests, the final 23 importer tests and 30 favorite-manager tests. The isolated original failures remain in `logs/sync-source-publication-isolated-reproduction.log`; final full-suite validation is rerun after these fixes.
- The controller persists operation intent before upload or import, including direction, configuration, generation and commit state; it removes the receipt only after terminal-state and configuration persistence settle. An applied task clears old pending edits by generation while retaining newer edits. In realtime mode, a queued edit that first finishes the old receipt also receives a separate upload for its new generation. Concurrent same/opposite-direction recovery requests share the original finalization; persistence retry after successful continuation does not invoke that continuation again.
- Configuration rolls back its old checkpoint only for confirmed `notApplied`; applied or uncertain outcomes retain the current configuration. Failed rollback retains its recovery work and prevents later finalization from writing the rejected draft again. Settings and implicit-state saves settle independently on both commit and rollback, preserving all four failures and original stacks.
- A persistent marker blocks replay of an unresolved operation after controller recreation or process restart. Corrupt markers also block manual, automatic and configuration admissions while retaining diagnostics. Remote filename/hash journaling and automatic reconciliation are not implemented, and in-process continuations cannot be reconstructed after restart. The marker provides conservative blocking, not complete crash recovery.
- Completed checks: zero strict-analysis diagnostics; Python 74 tests with 3 existing skips; structure, architecture (97 business entries), version and Git-dependency gates pass without broader exceptions. All 20 task-persistence tests pass, including an actual new-generation upload after recovery, shared concurrent finalization, restored/corrupt markers and queued-work blocking after an uncertain outcome. Artifacts remain outside the repository under `../venera_next_task_artifacts/2026-10-04-01a104a6/`; the focused log is `logs/data-sync-task-persistence-test.log`. **Final full-suite validation passes 2429 tests with 2 existing host skips and 61.08% LCOV; formatting for 25 Dart files, diff checks and Windows release pass.**

- Exit integration covers real-window read/platform waits, forced unmount, later-service failure recovery, work replacement and GlobalKey migration across windows. Selection and filename are captured before reading, fixing page-change renaming and duplicate extension dots. The photo page also releases its PageController.
- `readImageProvider` obtains the original key once, preserves custom stream creation/resolution and converts the first frame to PNG. Cancellation still joins key resolution, native conversion and underlying cleanup. The received ImageInfo remains owned until conversion ends; this consumer then releases its frame/subscription and removes a pending cache entry only after matching its actual completer. - `saveFile` uses `withSaveFileSource` to create a unique staging directory for bytes or a required source copy, retaining the suggested basename while containing its path within that operation. Success, cancellation, write failure and platform failure all await cleanup; simultaneous save/cleanup failures retain both errors and stacks. Chapterless sources use null for pages and the reader's `0` image-configuration convention. Resolved identity belongs to this read and does not mutate the favorite or its cache identity. Details and pages join the original JS Promise; synchronous conversion detaches model fields/collections before releasing result/error reference graphs. Res causes/stacks survive, and cancellation prevents subsequent page/image requests.

## P4/P5/P7: Visible-image and original chapter-task ownership (2026-10-05; committed as 03fd89f)

- `ReaderDisplayImageProvider` enrolls visible PhotoView/ComicImage subscriptions in the reader session while preserving the underlying provider's cache key. The relay does not enter global ImageCache. Preparation synchronously removes its own pending entry and subscription, then joins actual reads, release and already-started native frames. Other raw listeners keep owning shared work; completed caches remain. Every last-listener removal cancels relay work, including repeated listener cycles retained by an outer keepAlive.
- Holds do not publish cancellation errors to mounted plugins. Releasing the final hold re-subscribes after old cleanup finishes; failed relays support explicit retry and changing work owners isolates old sessions. The release API's `waitForCurrentFrame` snapshots current loading/native-frame Futures before waiting for post-frame release, preserving ordinary read failures and original stacks that arrive before cache handles retire. It joins only those original calls and cleanup, without waiting for codec closure, future frames or Flutter delivery deferred by a global hold. Other raw listeners retain ownership. Locally consumed cleanup errors are not replayed by future global preparation; an already-participating global preparation still independently receives them.
- `ReaderController` and `WaterfallController` retain actual completion of accepted tasks after UI cancellation, including beforeLoad, chapter reads and mode preparation. Main content resumes only attempts interrupted by session admission; ordinary supersession does not restart old work. Waterfall disposal waits for its current and retired tasks without closing shared imageWork.
- Replacing imageWork in the continuous view rebuilds the waterfall controller and clears old navigation/prepend-position flags. Original waits, queued frame callbacks and chapter notifications check controller identity and applicable revision, preventing revision reuse from letting old callbacks jump new content or clear new state; invalidated navigation callbacks cannot leave a permanent busy flag. Loading notifications retain their original callback and finish once: owner replacement releases the old notification, whereas unmount retires it without publishing ready over a main-content reload. The existing Host protocol keeps replacement-view readiness with main loading. Five new real-widget cases plus existing continuous input tests pass 9 cases; seven-mode auto-reading and real exit tests pass 28 cases.
- Chapter loading uses `RequestScope.runToCompletion`, retaining zone/CancelToken and joining the original call. FileSystemException after cancellation remains intact and does not start online fallback. Source pages use `runReadCodeToCompletion` to synchronously detach a Dart list; finally releases the original Promise's entire result/error reference graph by identity. Original errors, all cleanup failures and their stacks survive without retrying cleanup failures. Other `runReadCode` consumers retain their behavior.

The dimensions listener disposes each received ImageInfo clone after reading it. Three deprecated SDK calls receive equivalent replacements; other Dart changes are formatting only. The other 156 dependencies and SDK lock entries are unchanged, with no added business-dependency exceptions.
- Session recovery removes its own hold, then independently attempts image, auto-reading and duration recovery. Image recovery errors cannot skip other recovery, and failures retain original stacks. Windows Widget tests follow real ReaderImagesHost→Gallery/Continuous→relay→Base paths through pending raw file reads/native frames, back navigation, forced-unmount handoff and recovery of real images on the same Element/imageWork after a later window service fails. These are not physical-device evidence for other platforms.

## P4/P5: Image providers and prefetch ownership (2026-10-05)

- Image streams explicitly own their subscription and join the first cancel Future on success, cancellation and failure. Original read errors and stacks survive, cleanup failures aggregate separately, cancelled reads do not deliver bytes, and cleanup failure does not trigger fallback or retry.
- Cached/Reader/History/ImageFavorites/LocalComic providers check cancellation across file, directory, source-address and network stages while joining already-started original reads. Failure-path cancellation checks decide admission without replacing the original error. Image favorites do not refresh addresses or start cache writes after cancellation/cleanup failure. Direct save-image load(null, null) and existing local-favorite path/index issues remain separate follow-ups.
- Base providers expose a release join for their final consumer, covering loading, pending native frames and codec cleanup. ImageCache releases handles after a frame, so streams without listeners wait for a fresh post-frame callback before checking ownership; other listeners and real keepAlive handles retain their own work. Locally delivered cleanup failures are consumed from future global reporting, while an already-started global preparation retains its own failure.
- Late failure evicts only a pending cache entry whose completer still belongs to that load. Only ImageCache's pending lookup branch is used, avoiding orphan live handles created by querying a completed cache. Successful unlistened caches remain and an owned pending failure can load again. Live-only streams (for example after disabling cache or explicitly removing pending entries) remain with existing listeners; retry may require the final listener to release, so immediate retry for that branch is not claimed complete. Precache recursively unwraps preparation failures and deduplicates original errors by identity while retaining each original stack.
- Gallery and continuous views explicitly receive ReaderImageWork. Downloads and decoded prefetch enroll actual completion; replacement/disposal cancels only owned tasks, while back navigation, window preparation and detached-session handoff continue joining them. Precache releases its own pending cache entry/listener and then joins cleanup. Successful cache entries remain, and an old entry cannot evict a replacement completer under the same key. Ordinary online prefetch failure may be skipped; cleanup and real failures after cancellation belong to the session.
- Repository-wide references show no production constructor, tear-off or type use of LocalFavoriteImageProvider; its sole static caller deletes legacy covers. The class is removed in favor of pure-IO favorite_cover_cache, retaining the original path/hash, shared-reference rules and post-commit deletion timing. Existing favorites-manager cases (28) and remaining provider cases (17) pass. Historical symbol JSON records retirement without claiming a new full scan. Structural retirement checks cover the old path and the helper enters the business gate: 90→91 entries with no new dependency exception.
- Remaining scope: prefetch now owns cleanup when it becomes the final consumer; visible PhotoView/ComicImage streams that retain ownership still need their final unmount joined to the same reader session. Original WaterfallController chapter/SAF scan Futures, direct image saving, shared temporary files, DataSyncRemote/comic_backup, SAF/archives, final desktop core shutdown and five-platform/fixed-device performance acceptance remain unfinished. The original inventory stays 23 I / 28 P / 1 U.

- Final frozen-code validation: **all 2086 Windows Flutter tests pass**, strict analysis has zero diagnostics, and LCOV covers **21,154/36,015 lines (58.74%)**. Formatting for 24 changed/new Dart files, complete diff, structure/architecture/Git dependency and release-version checks pass. Python in UTF-8 mode reports 74 tests with 3 existing platform skips. Focused cases cover provider cancellation, real ImageCache final-handle release, native frame/codec cleanup, same-key replacement, error deduplication and reader back/unmount handoff. Input tests alternate real event-loop progress with test frames and delete files only after the owner releases, removing the fixed 50ms wait; all seven input cases pass.

Normal classification is delivered only after successful cleanup.
- The probe explicitly owns subscriptions and retains the first cancel Future, replacing the repeated StreamIterator.cancel path that could not join the first cancellation. Cancellation reentrant during listen still owns the resulting subscription. All samples finish before stages, original errors and stacks are reported together; descriptor and buffer release are attempted independently. Ordinary offline/corrupt samples can be skipped, while typed network/configuration cleanup failures and late cleanup events after bytes are delivered remain visible.
- ReaderState enrolls probes in existing `ReaderImageWork`. Session holds, back navigation, window closing and forced unmount join actual probe completion and accepted settings saves. Identical inputs reuse the result; force, chapter/image changes and same-chapter reloads that retain old images cancel the old probe. Retired work still drains without publishing to new inputs or clearing a replacement's busy state. Cancellation ends UI waiting without releasing actual task ownership.
- Samples and automatic mode changes are confirmed only after settings save succeeds. Failure does not roll back global settings because persistence may already be partial. Per-comic save revisions retain retry state across replaced probes, late failures and chapter changes. A newer successful save repairs older failures; an older result cannot hide a newer failure. The same error from result/done is recorded once by identity.
- File reads check cancellation after length lookup before admitting a byte read, while already-started reads retain their original Future. Integrity validation precedes the successful-read cancellation check, preserving FileSystemException for late empty/short reads. An owner can decline retries without replacing the original failure with cancellation; existing callers retain three attempts. No global test hooks or dependencies are added, and enforced business entries remain 90.
- This unit completes session ownership of layout probes. Other providers/high-level prefetch, shared temporary files, DataSyncRemote/comic_backup, SAF/archives, final desktop core shutdown and platform/performance acceptance remain in the original plan. Overall status stays 23 I / 28 P / 1 U.
- All 87 focused cases pass: probes (26), image tasks/file reads (18), automatic modes (30), and real exit combinations (13). Probe tests use real Flutter native objects and locally controlled creation, covering real local paths, first subscription cancellation, two-worker resource limits, late failures and shared-subscriber isolation. ReaderState tests use separate result/done/save gates; real reader/session/window combinations cover back waiting, root-reader saving and detached handoff. Controlled gates and Windows Widget combinations are not evidence for other physical platforms.

## P4/P5: Reader export and clipboard task ownership (2026-10-05)

- Each ReaderState creates `ReaderImageWork` and injects it into its session, exporter and gesture actions. Export and copy/menu-save share task registration. Cancellation stops the next stage, but acknowledges completion only after original selection/read/already-started platform Futures settle, removing the exporter's dependency on early-returning `RequestScope.run`. Reader instances remain independent, without a new global singleton or reset.
- Session holds synchronously freeze image admission and cancel active selection; independent holds compose. Recovery admits new actions without delivering cancelled old tasks. The selection overlay can reopen after cancellation. Scaffold explicitly receives imageWork, avoiding deactivated-ancestor lookup when disposal first initializes an exporter; changing the owner retires the old exporter while retaining its task registration.
- Original reads and platform deliveries drain alongside progress/duration writes, preserving immediate save admission and delaying sync notification until all finish. Direct disposal stops duration tracking synchronously. The existing reader return guard and window session preparation join the work; after forced widget removal, `WindowFrame.trackExitTask` still owns the session's final Future and prevents the window from skipping pending image operations.
- Real errors that can no longer reach their UI are retained with original errors/stacks; normal cancellation is not failure. If preparation has consumed image errors while saving remains pending, final disposal explicitly inherits those errors. New save revisions or a later successful image drain cannot erase them, and the same failure is not duplicated. Image/save failures aggregate independently; ordinary current-UI operation failures retain their existing error callback.
- Both `Share.shareFile` branches return and await the plugin Future. Adapter tests cover Windows/non-Windows parameters, waiting and asynchronous errors. The Windows plugin may return unavailable immediately after opening the share UI while a receiver reads its cache file later; plugin acknowledgment is therefore not permission to delete the file. Same-name temporary-file collisions/cleanup and `shareText` remain separate follow-ups.
- Clipboard copying preserves Windows/Linux rawRgba and macOS encoded-byte protocols. Finally attempts both image and codec release, retaining the first failure/stack and respecting the ByteData view. Real Flutter decoding and method-channel tests cover success, delayed platform completion, failure/retry, invalid input and frame decode failure after codec creation. Image creation/disposal hooks prove frame release; codec has no dedicated observation hook, so independent codec fault injection is not claimed.
- Focused tests cover image action/export/work (34), sessions (25), real readers (10), selection overlays (4), sharing (4) and clipboard (5). Combinations verify blocked return, pending platform delivery, recovery after later shutdown failure and detached-reader window waiting. ## P4/P7: WebDAV reuses the owned native request lifecycle (2026-10-05)

- The WebDAV library uses the endpoint's default `RHttpAdapter`, removing metadata-only `BufferedRHttpAdapter` and its duplicate private request mapping. The SDK still returns directory entries and complete UTF-8 text. Native completion, upload input/pump, cancellation and response subscriptions now share one owner instead of relying on the public bytes helper's incomplete upload drain.
- The transport tracks each configuration's adapter by identity. Disposal freezes admission and cancels SDK tokens before independently force-closing clients. Drain waits for every adapter even after one fails, then reports all original errors, stages and stacks. Failed drains retain registrations and remain failed on repeat waits; successful open-state drains retain clients for reuse after a cancelled exit.
- Real Dio cancellation after a partial response reproduced an unhandled cleanup error: Dio discards the response subscription's cancel Future, while the old onCancel propagated the same failure again. The cancellation branch now still joins all release work, with errors retained on the original `done` and adapter idle. Cancellation during release cannot finish early. Four regressions cover Dio/direct subscriptions before/during release, original errors/stacks and repeated waits.
- Ten WebDAV transport regressions pass, covering real SDK finite XML PROPFIND, Chinese paths/entries/eTag/time, chunked UTF-8 GET, 403, cancellation before headers/after partial body, peer disconnect and resumed reuse. Delayed configuration must settle without starting native work after cancellation. Controlled calls through two real adapters cover multiple failures, late cleanup and renewed tracking after an open-state drain. Combined image/network/WebDAV tests report 60 passes.
- URL, headers, proxy/DNS/TLS/redirect mappings remain intact. The shared stream exposes headers/chunks earlier, so custom-client progress and receiveTimeout timing may change. Current library operations only read directories/text; synthetic upload cleanup is not acceptance of large business uploads. Real cancellation still emits the previously observed FRB `Fail to post message to Dart` warning; actual completion uses the independent native Future. This unit changes no vendor/Rust source and does not claim that warning is gone.

## P4: Window placement persistence and mount handoff (2026-10-04–05)

- Move `WindowPlacement` and periodic persistence out of the window widget, removing the old static loop/cache/timer. The immutable model preserves the five `window_placement` JSON fields, default bounds and original nonnegative-origin rule. The tracker samples every 100ms, skips busy ticks without a backlog, serializes reads/writes and uses the destination captured by its native host.
- Main owns `WindowPlacementHost` and passes it to MyApp; native initialization runs once through a shared Future. Preserve ensureInitialized/preventClose/ready, title-bar/minimum-size configuration and platform ordering: Linux shows before applying bounds, while Windows/macOS apply before showing. Polling waits for the whole initialization chain and never starts after failure. Remounting only acquires a tracker instead of replaying ready's restore/unmaximize side effects.
- A new mount stops its predecessor synchronously and waits for every preceding drain. Disposing an intermediate mount before readiness cannot let a third mount bypass the oldest writer. Last valid bounds belong to the native host, including late reads from the prior mount, so maximized negative borders cannot overwrite them with defaults. Minimized checks before/after sampling prevent overwriting the saved maximized preference with false.
- Only successful writes confirm the cache. A failed overwrite may truncate the file, so it invalidates the saved cache and the next valid snapshot rewrites even when it equals an older value. The host retains unresolved writes across mounts; a minimized successor cannot falsely acknowledge them. A successful rewrite repairs the failure. Background errors remain reported per attempt, with bounded per-operation diagnostics for waiting callers.
- Preparation owns independent holds, stops ticks synchronously, joins accepted reads/writes and samples a final position. Failure permits recovery/retry; an old release cannot unlock other holds. Disposal joins real work, cancels the mount's wait for shared readiness and rejects late restart. InteractiveBindings owns placement on all desktops, keeps heartbeat active during preparation and aggregates preparation/recovery failures. Final watchdog closure drains its own calls independently so slow file writes cannot trigger an unfed timeout.
- Model/tracker enforcement increases business entries from 86 to 88 without new exceptions. Platform assembly still uses application logging/native adapters; method-channel tests do not establish real behavior on three desktops. Direct-overwrite persistence remains, without claims of power-loss atomicity or complete irreversible desktop-core shutdown. The remaining P0–P8 scope stays intact.
- Focused validation: 25 model/tracker, 16 host and 8 interactive tests. A new real WindowFrame/SyncWindowBinding regression joins slow reads/writes, resumes sampling after later preparation failure and closes successfully on retry; the window-binding file passes 29 tests. Host tests use real temporary partial writes, minimized successors and repair writes, with method-channel ordering checked for Windows/Linux/macOS.
- Windows release builds successfully. SHA-256: `VeneraNext.exe` = `C6A7D1C51D633ABFA1266A83634965AFC8D68E7276BED2840D2E85E0C06E5868`; `data/app.so` = `9711C1219FA30FBE51C812DDAFBD51F94CBD3C3B449FF387BCE42885BEA94E57`; the previously verified patched `rhttp.dll` remains `61FF5DB8A56122164AEF93D280D06C65921B41DAF6918BCD00B12E906823A96A`. External `window-placement-artifact-hashes.json` also confirms the packaged CHANGELOG matches source. This host remains Flutter 3.41.6/Dart 3.11.4, not declared-version 3.41.4 or other-platform evidence.
- Final frozen validation: **all 1924 Windows Flutter tests pass**, strict analysis has zero diagnostics, and LCOV covers **20,722/35,610 lines (58.19%)**. Formatting for 10 changed/new Dart files, complete diff, structure/architecture/Git dependency and release-version checks pass. Python in UTF-8 mode reports 74 tests with 3 existing platform skips. External logs: `logs/window-placement-{full-final,analyze-final,python-final,windows-release,window-widget}.log`; coverage: `coverage/window-placement-lcov.info`. ## P4/P7: Image pipeline draining and recoverable exit (2026-10-04)

- `SharedImageRequests` separately tracks shareable requests and retired requests still cleaning up; comic images, independent requests and thumbnails all participate. Cancellation joins actual `done`. Composable exit holds synchronously freeze admission and release idempotently; the next drain reports unconsumed cleanup failures. Automatic stream closure no longer returns an already-reported cancellation failure as an unhandled error.
- `BaseImageProvider` separates logical image streams from actual attempts and joins loading, processing, codec creation and native frame decoding during preparation. It retains Flutter's `MultiFrameImageStreamCompleter`, preserves late animation frames while publication is paused, and delivers them after recovery. Deferred images/codecs are released when listeners and keepAlive ownership truly end. Thumbnail slot waits are cancellable, and cleanup failures cannot trigger retries or fallbacks.
- Each image HTTP attempt owns its client, upload/response subscriptions and native completion signal. Public rhttp cancellation only signals cancellation; FRB stream `onDone` may precede Rust returning. The adapter joins the generated `executeNormal` Future captured through a per-request bridge before releasing resources. Retries drain the preceding attempt, configuration ownership spans the request, and original causes/all cleanup failures remain observable. Native failure also cancels an unfinished body observer to avoid an endless wait.
- An upstream rhttp 0.15.1 cancellation/response-or-error callback race reproduces a dropped FRB receiver panic using the public API; `panic=abort` terminates the process. The same version is vendored under `packages/rhttp`, with Rust `CallbackDrain` joining dispatched Dart acknowledgments before returning. No extra worker, full-response buffering or timer workaround is introduced. Package hash, MIT/Cargokit licenses and differences are documented in `packages/rhttp/LOCAL_PATCHES.md`. Fifteen upstream Dart files receive formatting only, verified by equal hashes after formatting separate copies identically; the sole upstream behavior change is in `rust/src/api/http.rs`.
- FRB 2.11.1 is now a direct dependency as well as an override; rhttp changes from hosted to the same-version path source. Root dependencies were resolved offline without version upgrades. All five native integrations build patched source and exclude upstream prebuilt artifacts; this configuration is not five-platform validation. Four Rust tests pass, and the public-API cancellation reproducer passes with the new DLL. CI adds locked Rust tests and platform-build triggers for `packages/`.
- Image scripts own a QuickJS engine per task, free returned/thrown reference graphs, close engine/port and join dispatch, retaining primary and cleanup errors. Image decode releases image/codec in reverse order in `finally` and honors ByteData offsets. Reader processing uses callbacks owned by their original engine, joining the original Future and asynchronous subscription cancellation.
- Window exit prepares providers and image requests concurrently after downloads and before history/sync persistence. Both branches settle even if one fails. Recovery resumes requests before providers; unmount waits for preparation before releasing holds.
- Enforced business entries increase from 79 to 86: image scripts, provider lifecycle, configuration, request scope, native requests and both shared-stream modules. Only inspected modules without transitive UI dependencies are enrolled, with no new exceptions. AppDio and image assembly still reach UI dependencies and remain outstanding.
- Boundaries remain: BufferedRHttpAdapter/WebDAV bytes upload pumps have no independent drain; other provider-internal late writes, layout probes, export/share/prefetch ownership, SAF/archives, desktop remount ownership and irreversible core shutdown are unfinished. P4.5/P7.2 remain partial; image preparation is not complete reader/native shutdown.
- Final validation: **1874 Windows Flutter tests pass**, strict analysis has zero diagnostics, and fresh LCOV covers **20,517/35,428 lines (57.91%)**. Formatting for 85 changed/new Dart files, structure/architecture/Git dependency and release-version checks pass. Python with `PYTHONUTF8=1` reports 74 tests and 3 existing platform skips; the initial default-GBK run with subprocess decoding errors is not final evidence. Production/tests stayed frozen during the full run, which used a fixed external DLL copy containing this source-built rhttp patch.
- Windows release builds successfully. The initial Cargokit online dependency resolution stalled; after ending that build, its generated runner was resolved offline from the existing mirror cache and precompiled before rebuilding. Only build_tool's path changed to the vendor directory, with no locked-version upgrades. The release/plugin rhttp DLL hashes match, and the public-API crash reproducer passes using that release DLL. FRB may still log `Fail to post message to Dart` after an error stream closes its port early; this warning is distinct from a native panic or failing to join native completion.

## P4/P7: Image configuration and JS callback ownership (2026-10-04)

- Comic images and thumbnails adopt the complete Map/List reference graph immediately after delivery, including unused callbacks, extension fields, map keys, cycles and aliases. Early cancellation, request construction, network/stream failure and normal completion all release it. Retry adopts the new configuration before retiring the old one; shared Dart references transfer once, while independently duplicated native wrappers are released separately even for the same JS function. Byte payloads do not require element-by-element scanning.
- Invalid callback results and thrown/rejected reference containers are released according to ownership, retaining aliases still borrowed by the current configuration until final disposal. Original errors/stacks and every cleanup failure remain observable. Cleanup failures cannot trigger a fallback retry or disappear behind cancellation. Cancellation joins the original resolver and started callback, cleaning late successful or rejected results.
- SourceImagesParser uses JsEngine.runOwnedCode; thumbnails retain synchronous results and also accept Promises. Synchronous returns/throws and Promise resolutions/rejections register references before publishing completion. Later callback calls retain their creating engine. Closing completes pending calls and releases references, with idempotent late frees and no access through a replacement runtime.
- Passing owned callbacks back to JS as arguments or thisVal borrows their original native references after runtime/liveness checks, preserving function identity, cycles, aliases and byte types. Native QuickJS regressions reproduced and fixed identity changes and leaked references caused by wrapping them as Dart functions. Independently duplicated reference graphs produced for Promise-rejection diagnostics are also released after logging.
- Native parser tests assert zero outstanding owned references before engine disposal so fallback engine cleanup cannot hide consumer leaks. Network tests use a local HTTP server and real Rhttp, covering repeated synchronous/asynchronous retries of the same JS function, cancellation immediately after delivery, cancellation during callbacks and multiple cleanup failures.
- P4.5/P7.2 remain partial: globally tracked retired requests, native image streams, decoding/export/prefetch, complete host/core shutdown, other source capabilities and five-platform/performance acceptance remain in scope. No release build was produced for this unit; the earlier heartbeat Windows release excludes this work and later reader changes.
- Final validation: all 1782 Windows Flutter tests passed with coverage; strict analysis has zero diagnostics. Formatting for 13 changed/new Dart files, diff, structure/architecture/Git dependency checks pass; the existing Python suite reports 68 tests with 3 platform skips. Fresh LCOV is 19,888/34,841 lines (57.08%), not device performance or overall acceptance. External evidence is logs/image-ownership-{final-full,final-analyze,python}.log and coverage/image-ownership-lcov.info. Source and tests stayed frozen during the final Flutter run. This host remains Flutter 3.41.6/Dart 3.11.4, not CI evidence for the declared version. A separate release_version.py false failure with local CRLF files was also found and is fixed separately from the image unit.

## P2: First service/navigation work unit (2026-09-30)

SourceUpdateService owns checking, downloading, cancellation, deduplication and repository validation. UI owns interaction; headless callers use the service. Tests inject its client factory. The source API is now enrolled in transitive UI checks; the old barrel remains compatible.

LocalComic no longer navigates. A pure policy resolves initial positions and a routing adapter obtains history and opens the reader. Tests explicitly preserve the existing grouped-download selection rule.

Restored the committed lockfile and ran offline dependency resolution with PUB_HOSTED_URL=https://pub.dev and --enforce-lockfile. Initial automatic resolution may have changed the local dependency environment, so the P0 result remains an observation; this locked run is the reproducible comparison point. No lockfile change is committed.

On Windows, prepend build/windows/x64/runner/Release to the test process PATH for the existing sqlite3.dll. Missing-library failures without that path were resolved by rerunning, not counted as passing skips. With locked dependencies and DLL PATH, flutter test --no-pub --coverage passed 577 tests with no skips/failures: six Channel tests removed, four reading-position tests added. Analysis has no errors/warnings and 24 infos; both dependency checks pass. Existing retry, duplicate update, failure and repository scenarios pass.

Device performance and other-platform validation remain pending. Services still depend on existing singletons; P4/P6 will address these. This does not claim all business domains are decoupled.

## P3: Typed reader runtime settings (2026-09-30)

Added immutable ReaderSettings with centralized effective values, defaults, numeric ranges, enum-string validation and legacy long-press compatibility. Manual comic mode remains independent of the other-settings switch; automatic preferences remain device/global scoped. Storage adapters preserve JSON and do not write during resolution. Global preloading/quick collection retain their scopes. Reader state, images, gestures and menus no longer use dynamic getters; CI prevents their return.

Six new behavior tests cover inheritance, modes, legacy values, invalid inputs, immutable snapshots and storage preservation. One new architecture gate test was added. With locked dependencies and Windows DLL PATH, 583 Flutter tests passed without failures/skips; analysis reports no errors/warnings and the existing 24 infos. Python suite: 52 tests, 3 platform skips, no failures.

Only architectural changes were staged in files shared with existing user edits; menu locking, long-press pause and related settings changes remain uncommitted. P3 is not fully complete: form writes, a single source of defaults, and sync/network/appearance configuration remain to migrate before P4.

## P3: Reader forms and write boundary (2026-09-30)

ReaderPreferences now owns reader defaults, validation and slider ranges/steps, shared by initial storage and runtime snapshots. The legacy long-press null default remains for migration. Typed bindings connect switches, selectors, sliders, reading-mode forms and quick brightness writes. Other domains retain compatible controls; existing scopes and integer slider storage are preserved.

Added four store/scope/default tests and one form architecture gate. With locked dependencies and Windows DLL PATH, all 587 Flutter tests passed without skips/failures. Reader forms/defaults are complete. Sync/network/appearance configuration and further compatibility cleanup remain within P3; P4–P8 and device/platform verification are not complete.

## Download queue write lifecycle fix (2026-09-30)

- P3 regression testing exposed a Windows race between temporary-directory deletion and background persistence after cancellation. Saves now capture the path and snapshot, run in order, and expose completion. Failures reach awaiting callers and are logged without blocking later saves.
- Tests await actual persistence and cover failure recovery and consecutive snapshots. The working tree, including pending settings migration and user edits, passed 592 Flutter tests; analysis reported no errors/warnings and 24 existing infos. This fix is committed separately from settings migration.

All 54 Python tests completed successfully (three platform skips); structure, architecture and Git dependency checks passed.

## P3: App sync configuration (2026-09-30)

- Pure SyncConfiguration/SyncConnection parsing distinguishes empty and malformed connections and retains credential whitespace until the WebDAV boundary. SyncPreferenceStore centralizes configuration, mode, interval, pending and last-attempt access; rollback preserves raw legacy values.

A subsequently added regression for local edits during failed configuration passed with all 13 schedule tests; the full suite was not repeated for that test-only addition. ## P4: Initialization concurrency and failure contract (2026-09-30)

- Init now shares one attempt Future and exposes four lifecycle states. Concurrent initialization/waiters receive the same success or original error. ensureInit still waits for explicit startup; failures remain cached until retryInit. Retry during active/ready work does not duplicate execution.
- Audited all four production ensureInit sites: source repositories and favorites wait for Appdata, the source manager waits for JS, and the local library waits for the source manager. Appdata/JS initialization does not wait back on these managers. Startup error policy and mode separation remain a subsequent unit.
- JS initialization cleans up engine/port resources and propagates failures instead of pretending to be ready. Added three Init tests and one native JS failure/retry test.
- All 600 Flutter tests passed without failures/skips. Analysis: zero errors/warnings, 24 existing infos. Structure, architecture and Git dependency checks passed. Existing user changes remain separate.
- This completes only the initialization-contract unit; core/interactive/headless startup, listener/timer ownership and session requests remain pending in P4.

## P4: Sync startup and window lifecycle (2026-09-30)

- DataSync construction no longer registers listeners, starts timers or touches a window. The runtime explicitly starts it once. Disposal is idempotent, rejects new requests, prevents queued transfers from starting and suppresses late notifications; active transfers finish to avoid interrupting persistence.
- SyncWindowBinding owns the upload-wait/force-exit window interaction, registers/removes its close listener with the widget lifecycle, coalesces repeated close requests and does not exit after unmounting. The interactive entry point supplies the exit action.
- Removed debugDisableWindowCloseHandler and the test-only wait wrapper; tests use waitForUpload. A separate staged viewport-test variant preserves the user's existing edits.
- Added four lifecycle/window tests. All 604 Flutter tests passed on Windows without failures/skips. The two custom-window-button tests explicitly skip other platforms; real platform manual acceptance remains unverified. Analysis: no errors/warnings, 24 existing infos. Structure and architecture checks passed.
- The original startup entry still calls start; core/interactive/headless startup separation follows. Sync dependency injection/instances, other service timers and reader request ownership remain pending.

## P4: Core and startup-mode separation (2026-09-30)

- CoreBootstrap caches startup and orders environment, settings, infrastructure, sources, stores and completion. Source failure prevents dependent stores from waiting indefinitely. Shared bootstrapCore does not mount UI or activate automatic sync, window heartbeat or share navigation. Interactive init retains its entry point and caches startup.
- Paths/settings, cookie/HTTP, OpenCC, JS/sources and database failures stop startup. App/tag translations retain original-text fallback; SAF remains optional with logged errors. Failed startup requires process restart rather than implicitly reopening partially initialized databases. Interactive failure uses the existing top-level logger; headless failure emits a structured error and exits 1.
- Headless source UI explicitly reports unsupported operation without using Navigator or launching UI. Cookie creation accepts the resolved data directory, allowing isolated testing without personal application data.
- Added three orchestration/headless tests, one real Windows core integration test and one boundary test. The real core initialized SQLite, HTTP, JS and cache under a temporary directory without a Widget tree; empty source data did not create an automatic sync instance.
- All 608 Flutter tests passed on Windows without failures/skips. Analysis: no errors/warnings, 24 existing infos. Python: 56 successful tests (three platform skips); structure, architecture and Git dependency checks passed. Native CLI subprocess and other-platform startup checks remain pending; the core native integration test explicitly skips non-Windows.
- Core still imports some legacy domain barrels; transitive UI isolation is not complete. Interactive listener/heartbeat ownership, remaining service instances and reader request ownership continue in P4.

## P4: Interactive event subscriptions and heartbeat (2026-09-30)

- The mounted app owns InteractiveBindings, explicitly starts it and disposes Android link/share subscriptions and Windows heartbeat on unmount. Repeated start is idempotent; partial attach failure cleans up existing subscriptions. App disposal also removes the lifecycle observer and global rebuild callback.
- EventSubscription has two production consumers: links and text sharing. It serializes awaited handling, reports errors and owns disposal. Handlers check lifetime after awaits, dropping buffered events and suppressing late navigation. The global text-share initialization flag is removed.
- Added five behavior tests covering ordering, disposal during awaits, recovery after handler errors, repeated start, timer cancellation, late heartbeat and partial attach failure. Timer tests control the clock boundary without a Widget tree or real waits.

## P4: Automatic sync scheduling ownership (2026-09-30)

- The mounted app starts/stops BackgroundSync, which owns the 15-minute WebDAV check timer and DataSync scheduling. The static WebDAV timer/start method is removed; initialization no longer activates automatic synchronization.
- DataSync distinguishes stop from dispose: stop suspends scheduling while continuing to observe local changes for pending state and allowing requested transfers to finish. Dispose removes observation and rejects further requests. Restart catches up overdue work; repeated start/stop is idempotent.
- Generations isolate stale timer callbacks, partial startup failure stops automatic synchronization, and core/headless checks also forbid BackgroundSync. Existing WebDAV transfers retain their commit behavior; transfer cancellation was not changed in this unit.
- Added four behavior tests covering stale ticks, restart deduplication, startup cleanup, pending changes from real Appdata saves while stopped, and active transfer completion. All 617 Flutter tests passed on Windows. Analysis: zero errors/warnings, 24 existing infos. Python: 56 successful tests (three platform skips); structure/architecture checks passed.
- DataSync observation and legacy managers still have process-level singleton dependencies, to be injected/instantiated in P6. Follow updates, cache scanning and reader request lifetimes remain unfinished.

## P4/P2: Follow-update service and notification boundary (2026-09-30)

- FollowUpdatesService moved out of the page with injected folder/busy lookup, download waiting, task creation, notification and error handling. FollowUpdateTask exposes only update counts and cancellation. Background checks cancel their own task handle rather than unrelated foreground work. Explicit user-requested disable still cancels active checking globally.
- Separate attempt objects isolate waiting/running/cancelled/restarted checks; old completion cannot clear new work or notify late. The mounted application starts/stops the service, favorites callback and sync listener.
- Page/preview widgets now use ordinary State with explicit domain notification subscription/disposal, removing AutomaticGlobalState, state keys and GlobalState lookup. Multiple previews update together; manual checks and favorites changes use the same notification path.
- Added follow_updates_api.dart for the service/task contract as the second business entry protected by transitive UI checks. Legacy manager/page/runtime barrels remain for compatibility; whole-domain transitive UI isolation is not claimed.
- Added four service tests without global reset hooks and one multi-preview/disposal Widget test. All 622 Flutter tests passed on Windows without failures/skips. Analysis: no errors/warnings, 24 existing infos. Structure/architecture checks passed; the existing aggregate dependency cycle did not grow.

## P4/P6: Cache scan and shutdown lifecycle (2026-09-30)

- CacheManager.open takes explicit data/cache paths and a scanner. Construction opens storage without launching asynchronous scanning; the compatibility singleton also resolves paths only once. Runtime explicitly starts the manager-owned scan without blocking UI startup.
- cache_scan returns results only. Applying results uses the owning instance and rechecks tracked file ownership, removing global CacheManager access from scan callbacks. Scanning, reads/writes, eviction and clearing share an ordered queue so stale results cannot overwrite new writes/clears. Write input is copied when queued.
- Dispose rejects new work and drains accepted operations before closing SQLite. Failed operations do not poison later work. Scan failure preserves tracked size and reports the error. File layout/schema remain unchanged; cache tests no longer mutate App paths or global scan/cleanup hooks.
- Replaced two tests with seven instance-based behavior tests covering real scans, error recovery, ordering, draining close, eviction and path isolation. Core integration uses the real disposal API. All 627 Flutter tests passed on Windows without failures/skips. Analysis: no errors/warnings, 24 existing infos; structure/architecture/Git dependency checks passed.
- Cache operations now wait behind an active scan; large-cache first-read latency and throughput require P6 performance validation. Compatibility singleton callers will narrow with their respective domains; reader request ownership in P4 and P5–P8 remain unfinished.

## P4: Chapter request ownership (2026-10-01)

- ReaderImagesState and ContinuousModeState each own a chapter request scope, cancelled and disposed on unmount. Ordinary chapter changes reuse the existing chapter key to recreate state; waterfall chapter requests belong to their view.
- Each chapter load creates a child scope, propagates it to source calls through the Zone, and detaches it on completion. Cancellation releases the caller immediately. Checks after local reads and online responses prevent late recovery callbacks, preserving local-first resolution, stable chapter IDs and missing-file fallback.
- Three new behavioral tests cover pre-cancellation, stalled requests and late callbacks, and independent owners. All 630 Windows Flutter tests pass; analysis has zero errors/warnings and 24 existing infos; structure, architecture and Git dependency checks pass.

## P4: Image predownload subscriptions and stalled stream cancellation (2026-10-01)

- Gallery and continuous reader views own ReaderImageDownloads, replacing predownloads that created streams without consuming them. Pending work is deduplicated by image/source/comic/chapter; completion and failure release handles. Disposal rejects new work and waits for owned subscriptions to cancel. Delayed continuous predownloads no longer access context after unmount.
- readImageStream is shared by predownloads and ReaderImageProvider. It races StreamIterator events against cancellation, releasing stalled subscriptions while preserving other consumers. Final bytes and errors also release subscriptions. Custom image-processing Future branches without onCancel now observe the stop signal and check cancellation before publishing results.

## P4: Cancellation of shared image requests (2026-10-01)

- SharedRequestStream is extracted from the image module and owns a RequestScope independent of any caller. Obtaining a stream does not start its source; listening does. Only active subscribers retain work. Last release signals cancellation before cancelling the asynchronous source. Factory errors, natural completion and repeated cancellation close once; generator termination errors during cancellation no longer escape unhandled.
- Shared source configuration runs in the independent scope, and HTTP explicitly receives its cancellation token. Cancellation checks guard cache/network results, stream data, cache writes and retries. Cache hits now return immediately, avoiding source resolution and network work after fully consuming cached bytes. The unwrapped entry used by direct downloads retains its existing independent behavior.

## P4: Decoded prefetch and reader exit isolation (2026-10-01)

- Gallery views own ReaderImagePrecache, deduplicating pending decoded prefetches by ReaderImageProvider and retaining release callbacks. Successful images stay attached through frame end; unmount/errors release listeners. The manager explicitly relies on ReaderImageProvider resolving synchronously to itself as the cache key, rather than generalizing to asynchronous-key providers.

Flutter releases the final keep-alive handle at frame end, then the underlying subscription is cancelled. ReaderImagesState.dispose no longer calls cancelAllLoadingImages, so exiting a reader does not globally cancel other views' downloads.

## P5: Reader navigation controller (2026-10-01)

- ReaderController owns page/chapter, pending page, end-page jump flags and navigation generations. ## P5: Waterfall chapter coordination (2026-10-01)

## P5: Clock and battery status lifecycle (2026-10-01)

- ReaderStatusInfo is extracted from scaffold with injected clock/battery readers; the default adapter uses Battery and publishes ReaderBatterySnapshot. Clock and battery share one one-second timer, reads do not overlap within a dependency generation, and outlined text rendering is shared.
- Disposal cancels scheduling; dependency replacement advances the generation and rejects late results/errors. Null means unsupported and stops battery polling while the clock continues. Transient failures retain the previous value and retry on the next tick. Platform Futures cannot be forcibly aborted; the component stops scheduling and ignores invalid results.
- Explicit behavior corrections: an initial 0% is no longer treated as absent hardware, charging icons update even at unchanged charge level, and polling failures no longer escape unhandled. Clock formatting, icon thresholds, outlines and shell placement remain.
- Four injected widget tests cover stalled reads/disposal, recovery, charging/zero level, dependency replacement, unsupported hardware and independent clock updates. All 699 Windows Flutter tests passed; analysis has no errors/warnings and 24 existing infos; structure, architecture and Git dependency checks passed.
- Only scaffold architecture edits were staged; original menu/pause changes remain unstaged. Native battery behavior still needs device validation. Top menu, settings, image actions, session/platform adapters and P6–P8/performance acceptance remain unfinished.

ReaderImageSelection captures source/comic/chapter identity, chapter/image numbers and title before reading; completion no longer accesses context for filenames. Existing naming/MIME rules remain.
- Each export owns a child RequestScope. Disposal cancels selection/read waits and suppresses late results; already-open platform operations finish independently, and underlying file Futures cannot be forcibly stopped. Current read/platform errors are reported and later attempts can recover. The unused selectImageToData entry is removed.

## P5: Orientation ownership and application scope (2026-10-01)

- ReaderOrientationCoordinator owns plain handles instead of a static Widget State list. Only the top handle can cycle; releasing it restores the underlying lock, and releasing the last returns to system policy. Scopes share no state and disposed scopes cannot acquire or reactivate handles.

## P6: Asynchronous write copies and lifetime ownership (2026-10-01)

- History.copy detaches all fields and read keys. Progress/duration APIs capture the copy, database path and generation when submitted, preventing later caller mutation from changing queued values. Duration completion updates the caller only if its identity still matches.
- Cache completion reloads actual persisted data instead of caching unsubmitted caller changes. Only the current initialized generation caches/notifies. Closing invalidates callbacks; reopening cannot redirect accepted writes to another database. Old operations may still finish on their captured database and are not cancelled.

## P5/P6: Drain progress before exit synchronization (2026-10-01)

The existing synchronous exit-storage adapter remains; this unit does not resolve synchronous/asynchronous database write races or change final desktop shutdown behavior.

## P6: Complete favorite identities and snapshot merging (2026-10-01)

- Favorite references and tracking sets now use complete (id, type) keys, preventing XOR collisions from conflating counts, membership and update state. ## P6: Favorite initialization and connection close (2026-10-01)

- Initialization registers its Future before execution. Concurrent/ready calls for the same path reuse it, while failure releases resources and allows a later explicit init retry. Changing data paths requires close. Appdata readiness precedes local-connection migration/default creation and publication, removing partial-initialization notifications through public createFolder.
- Close is repeatable, clears the connection/counts/identity/tracking indexes and invalidates old initialization generations. Closing after the initializer body finishes but before its Future is delivered also reports failure; late failure cannot dispose a reopened connection. clearAll closes and rebuilds using the original connection path, avoiding deletion at a changed global path, but does not yet drain all in-flight isolates.

## P6: Composable repository transactions and savepoints (2026-10-01)

## P4/P6: Freeze storage admission and drain accepted imports on exit (2026-10-02)

- LocalComicStorageGuard tracks completion at import admission, including imports waiting for migration. Exit preparation rejects new imports/exclusive operations and drains accepted imports plus migration/recovery. Completion signals do not swallow operation failures, which still reach their original callers. Preparation is shared and release identity prevents stale callbacks from lifting later restrictions.
- New local_import_lifecycle.dart composes PDF cancellation/cleanup with general storage draining. SyncWindowBinding uses this public entry before downloads, history and upload. PDF batches stop first so queued cancellation cannot start another item after storage freezes.

PDF documents rejected before conversion are disposed; entered conversions retain their existing cleanup ownership.

## P4/P6: Awaitable deletion, storage exclusion and current paths (2026-10-02)

- Single/chapter/batch deletion returns a Future under runWithExclusiveStorage, rejecting active imports or queued downloads. Empty queues freeze admissions and drain cancellation cleanup until deletion finishes. Paths come from current registered records rather than stale page objects; missing identities are no-ops.
- File-deletion isolates return completion and history deletion is awaited. Batch SQL and filesystem failures propagate instead of silently reporting completion. UI confirmations await success, show errors and remove arbitrary chapter-deletion delay. Cross-database/filesystem rollback is not implemented; earlier side effects may commit before later cleanup fails.
- Queue suspend/releaseSuspension optionally suppress notifications; only confirmed-empty exclusive-storage calls use this to avoid two spurious library notifications. Normal download-exit notifications remain. Initial regressions exposed this difference and the original deletion notification contract now passes.
- A real SQLite/directory regression covers rejection of all three deletion kinds during import, current versus stale paths and completed file cleanup. Final analysis: no errors/warnings, 23 existing infos; structure, architecture, 12 script tests and Git dependencies passed.

## P4/P6: WebDAV runtime instances and request sessions (2026-10-02)

- `WebDavLibrarySource` receives settings reading, cache and transport through its constructor. Snapshot/in-flight caches, notifiers and sync tasks are instance-owned. Disposal does not wait for uncancellable custom-transport Futures; late completions must pass session checks.

Connection changes (including passwords and external imports) invalidate old sessions; new synchronization need not wait for old requests. Stale indexes/snapshots/chapter/metadata results cannot overwrite current cache/status. Schedule-only changes retain valid sessions.

- Remaining limits: concurrent clients, same-name overwrites and lost server responses are not remotely atomic; cancellation cannot undo sent operations. ## P4/P7: JS dialog callback and resource lifetime (2026-10-03)

## P4/P6/P8: sync-window waiting and exit-guard cleanup (2026-10-03)

- SyncWindowBinding owns its upload loading controller and closes it in finally after completion/failure. Shutdown waiting sets false: its button can force exit, but back/disposal cannot be mistaken for that action. Close checks Navigator.mounted and route.isActive. ## P5: Content-load orchestration belongs to the reader controller (2026-10-03)

- ReaderController.loadContent owns deduplication, preparation/fetch/mode-preparation phases and success/failure commits. Ownership is checked at each asynchronous boundary; replaced/disposed attempts return ignored without advancing or replacing newer content.
- ReaderImages wires local migration, chapter loading and mode preparation, then presents ready/failed/ignored outcomes. Migration now checks scope cancellation after its wait, preventing stale mounted tasks from restoring old positions or starting new requests. History/content-ready notifications retain their microtask timing.
- Ten new controller tests cover deduplication, loading until preparation ends, retry after each phase fails, and replacement/disposal during each phase. All 19 targeted and 1252 full Flutter tests passed. Logs: output/reader-load-{targeted,full,analyze}.log.

Already-started mode probes keep their existing lifecycle ownership; this controller rejects stale content commits without rolling back external effects. Existing user reader changes are preserved and excluded from this commit.

## P5: Viewport attachment ownership and host transfer (2026-10-03)

## P5: Reader sidebar requests and interaction lifecycle (2026-10-03)
