## P4/P5/P6: Reading-save commit outcomes and recoverable exit (2026-10-04)

- PersistenceFailure distinguishes notCommitted, committed and unknown. Duration storage permits replay only before a write or after confirmed rollback; failed rollback and unknown worker outcomes are never replayed automatically. A post-commit cleanup error still publishes the increment to the item/cache/listeners, retaining both the original cleanup error and any publication error. Connection configuration plus cleanup failure retains both diagnostics; ordinary SQLite configuration exceptions keep their type.
- ReaderHistoryWriter retains failures by submission revision: a newer successful snapshot repairs old failures, while an older success cannot mask a newer failure. Flush joins progress arriving during its wait. ReadingSessionTracker retains individual failed periods and retries only explicitly uncommitted ones. Committed, unknown and error-reporter failures remain observable. Both final writer disposals share their success or failure Future.
- ReaderSession adds independent composable holds and recoverable preparation, preserving actual foreground/content readiness while held. Resuming starts a fresh clock without counting shutdown waiting. Save, notification and resume errors are aggregated; progress arriving after its writer drained while another was pending is drained again. One batch is notified once, and new changes after resuming notify sync again. Final disposal closes admission and joins preparation.
- WindowFrame synchronously tells sessions to stop time after guards pass and input freezes, before any await. ReaderExitGuard connects the real ReaderState toolbar, gesture menu, system back and native window reader-pop path: save before navigation, freeze pointer/keyboard/accessibility actions while waiting, and compose ancestor navigation admission. Failure keeps the page and restores focus, allowing retry or an explicit leave-without-saving action. Volume navigation also respects session holds.
- Ordinary widget removal registers the original failure Future with window tracking; separate logging no longer converts it to success. Failed preparation releases its own hold without unlocking independent owners or later attempts. Leaving without saving is available only after a failed attempt and never silently replays uncertain increments or removes diagnostics.
- This does not complete all reader asynchronous resources or desktop core shutdown. Image/source/decoding/export/prefetch draining, fully stopping late content producers, remount ownership, irreversible core shutdown, SAF/Rhttp, five-platform and fixed-device performance acceptance remain in scope. A navigation guard does not prove recovery after forced process termination.
- Final review added sync notification after a same-revision save retry, a session hold throughout explicit-leave reverse animation, and a new drain for progress arriving after preparation completed. Window tracking joins every late save and releases all successful preparation holds. Six real ReaderState exit scenarios, 21 ReaderSession cases and 20 exit-guard cases pass. Following ui-ux-pro-max focus/navigation guidance, real Segoe UI rendering checked light/dark themes at 375×700, 500×800 and 700×375 with 3.2× text and reduced motion; six screenshots under the external reader-exit/ directory show no clipping or overflow.
- Final validation: both full Windows Flutter runs, including final coverage, passed all 1706 tests. Strict analysis has zero diagnostics; formatting for 19 changed/new Dart files, diff, structure/architecture/Git dependency checks pass. Python reports 68 tests with 3 existing skips. Fresh LCOV is 19,585/34,601 lines (56.60%), neither a performance metric nor an overall acceptance threshold, and not a direct comparison with older coverage over different source scope. Source and tests stayed unchanged throughout final coverage. External evidence: logs/reader-save-recovery-{final-full,final-coverage,verified-analyze,python}.log and coverage/reader-save-recovery-lcov.info; the first analysis retains a subsequently fixed null-element style diagnostic. This host remains Flutter 3.41.6/Dart 3.11.4, not proof for the declared repository version or other platforms.
